({}^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}},\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is a cotorsion pair,
it suffices to show
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))^{\perp}.

In fact, since
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
are cotorsion pairs,
it follows that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\binom{\mathcal{U}^{\perp}}{\mathcal{V}^{\perp}}
.
Since by assumption
\operatorname{Tor}^{A}_{1}(M,\ \mathcal{U})=0=\operatorname{Tor}^{B}_{1}(N,\ \mathcal{V})
, it follows from
Lemma
3.9
(1) that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\perp}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})^{\perp}=({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\ \cup\ {\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}))^{\perp}.

Thus

\displaystyle(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))^{\perp}

\displaystyle=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\perp}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right))^{\perp}=\{^{\perp}[({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\ \cup\ {\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}))^{\perp}]\}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\ \cup\ {\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}))^{\perp}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

here one uses the fact
(^{\bot}(\mathcal{S}^{\bot}))^{\bot}=\mathcal{S}^{\bot},
for any class
\mathcal{S}
of modules.

If
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
are hereditary, then
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are closed under taking the cokernels of monomorphisms.
By the construction of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, it is clear that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is also closed under taking the cokernels of monomorphisms, i.e.,

({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is hereditary.

Conversely, let
({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
be hereditary.
Using functors

{\rm Z}_{A}
and
{\rm Z}_{B}
, one sees that
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are closed under taking the cokernels of monomorphisms, i.e.,
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
are hereditary.

(2)
Similarly, it suffices to show
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\ ^{\perp}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

In fact, by Lemma
3.9
(2) one has

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}^{\perp}\mathcal{X}\\
{}^{\perp}\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\ ^{\perp}{\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})=\ ^{\perp}({\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cup\ {\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})).

Thus

\ {}^{\perp}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})

\displaystyle=\ ^{\perp}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}^{\perp}\mathcal{X}\\
{}^{\perp}\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})=\ ^{\perp}\{[^{\perp}({\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cup\ {\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))]^{\perp}\}

\displaystyle=\ ^{\perp}({\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cup\ {\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

here one uses the fact
{}^{\bot}((^{\bot}\mathcal{S})^{\bot})=\ ^{\bot}\mathcal{S},
for any class
\mathcal{S}
of modules.

If
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
are hereditary, then
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
are closed under taking the kernels of epimorphisms.
By construction,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is also closed under taking the kernels of epimorphisms, i.e.,

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
is hereditary. One can see the converse, by using functors

{\rm Z}_{A}
and
{\rm Z}_{B}
.
\square

3.5.
Induced isomorphisms between
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}
(continued)

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
,
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
a
\Lambda
-module.

(1)
If
g
is a monomorphism, then
\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}({\rm C}_{A}L,\ X)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{A}X)
,
\forall\ X\in A\mbox{\rm-Mod}
.

(2)
If
f
is a monomorphism, then
\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}({\rm C}_{B}L,\ Y)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{B}Y)
,
\forall\ Y\in B\mbox{\rm-Mod}
.

(3)
If
\widetilde{f}
is an epimorphism, then
\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}({\rm Z}_{A}X,\ L)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(X,\ {\rm K}_{A}L)
,
\forall\ X\in A\mbox{\rm-Mod}
.

(4)
If
\widetilde{g}
is an epimorphism, then
\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}({\rm Z}_{B}Y,\ L)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(Y,\ {\rm K}_{B}L)
,
\forall\ Y\in B\mbox{\rm-Mod}
.

Proof.

We only prove (1) and (3). The assertions (2) and (4) can be similarly proved.

(1) Taking an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}K_{1}\\
K_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{s,t}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1}\\
i_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P_{1}\\
P_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p_{1}\\
p_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\longrightarrow 0

with
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P_{1}\\
P_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}
a projective module, one gets a commutative diagram with exact rows:

\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}K_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes i_{2}}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}P_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes p_{2}}
\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{1}}
\textstyle{P_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Since
g
is a monomorphism, by Snake Lemma

0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Coker}t\longrightarrow\operatorname{Coker}v\longrightarrow\operatorname{Coker}g\longrightarrow 0

is exact. Since
P=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P_{1}\\
P_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}
is projective,

\operatorname{Coker}v={\rm C}_{A}P
is a projective
A
-module.

Consider adjoint pair
({\rm C}_{A},\ {\rm Z}_{A})
between
\Lambda
-Mod and
A
-Mod. (Note that
{\rm C}_{A}
is not exact.)
Applying Lemma
3.7
(1) to
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
, one gets

\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}({\rm C}_{A}L,\ X)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{A}X),\ \forall\ X\in A\mbox{\rm-Mod}.

(3) Similarly, taking an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma_{1}\\
\sigma_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I_{1}\\
I_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi_{1}\\
\pi_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}C_{1}\\
C_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{s,t}\longrightarrow 0

with
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I_{1}\\
I_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}
an injective module, one gets a commutative diagram with exact rows:

\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\sigma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}I_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{2}}
\textstyle{I_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\textstyle{C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Using adjoint isomorphism, one gets a commutative diagram with exact rows:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}}
\textstyle{I_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{u}}
\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{s}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(M,\sigma_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,I_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(M,\pi_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,C_{2})}

Since
\widetilde{f}
is an epimorphism, by Snake Lemma that

0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{u}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{s}\longrightarrow 0

is exact. Since
I=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I_{1}\\
I_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}
is injective,

\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{u}={\rm K}_{A}I
is an injective
A
-module.

Consider adjoint pair
({\rm Z}_{A},\ {\rm K}_{A})
between
A
-Mod and
\Lambda
-Mod. Applying Lemma
3.7
(2)
to
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
, one gets

\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}({\rm Z}_{A}X,\ L)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(X,\ {\rm K}_{A}L),\ \forall\ X\in A\mbox{\rm-Mod}.

∎

3.6.
Key lemmas for Theorem
3.2

The following lemma will play an important role in the proof of Theorem
3.2
.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
,
\mathcal{X}\subseteq A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, and
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

(1)
If
\mathcal{X}\supseteq\ _{A}\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{Y}\supseteq\ _{A}\mathcal{I}
, then

\Delta({}^{\perp}\mathcal{X},\ {}^{\perp}\mathcal{Y})=\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}).

(2)
If
\mathcal{X}\supseteq\ _{A}\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Y}\supseteq\ _{B}\mathcal{P}
, then
\nabla(\mathcal{X}^{\perp},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\perp})={\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})^{\perp}.

Proof.

(1) Let
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in\Delta({}^{\perp}\mathcal{X},\ {}^{\perp}\mathcal{Y})
.
By definition
f
and
g
are monomorphisms, and
\operatorname{Coker}f\in{}^{\perp}\mathcal{Y}
and
\operatorname{Coker}g\in{}^{\perp}\mathcal{X}
.
Since
g
is a monomorphism and
\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}({\rm C}_{A}L,\ \mathcal{X})=\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(\operatorname{Coker}g,\ \mathcal{X})=0
, it follows from Lemma
3.10
(1) that

\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X}))=0
, i.e.,
L\in\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})
.
Similarly, since
f
is a monomorphism and
\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}({\rm C}_{B}L,\ \mathcal{Y})=\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(\operatorname{Coker}f,\ \mathcal{Y})=0
, by Lemma
3.10
(2),

\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))=0
, i.e.,
L\in\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
. Thus,
L\in\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
.

Conversely, let
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
, i.e.,

\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X}))=0=\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))
.

Claim 1:

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(g,\ X):\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(L_{1},\ X)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N\otimes_{B}L_{2},\ X)
is an epimorphism,
for any module
X\in\mathcal{X}
. In fact, for any
A
-map
u:N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\longrightarrow X
,
consider
A
-map
g^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u\\
g\end{smallmatrix}\right):N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\longrightarrow X\oplus L_{1}
and
the exact sequence of
A
-modules

0\longrightarrow X\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}X\oplus L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{(0,1)}L_{1}\longrightarrow 0.

Put
f^{\prime}=(0,f):M\otimes_{A}(X\oplus L_{1})\longrightarrow L_{2}.
Then
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\oplus L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}}
is indeed a
\Lambda
-module.
We stress that this is a place where one needs the assumption
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
:
Given any
U\in A\mbox{-Mod}
and
V\in B\mbox{-Mod}
, for arbitrary
u\in\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M\otimes_{A}U,V)
and
v\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N\otimes_{B}V,U)
,

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\\
V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}
is always a left
\Lambda
-module, since the conditions

v(1_{N}\otimes u)=0
and
u(1_{M}\otimes v)=0
automatically hold.

Then one can check that

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\oplus L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(0,1)\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\longrightarrow 0

is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules. Since
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}={\rm Z}_{A}X\in{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})
and
L\in\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
,
this exact sequence splits. Thus there is a
\Lambda
-map

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)\\
\beta\end{smallmatrix}\right):\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\oplus L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f^{\prime},g^{\prime}}

such that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(0,1)\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
b\end{smallmatrix}\right)\\
\beta\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm Id}_{L}
.
So
b={\rm Id}_{L_{1}}
and
\beta={\rm Id}_{L_{2}}
. Thus one gets a commutative diagram

\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u\\
g\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{X\oplus L_{1}}

and hence
u=ag
. This proves
Claim 1
.

Claim 2:

g
is a monomorphism. In fact, embedding
N\otimes_{B}L_{2}
into an injective
A
-module one has a monomorphism
i:N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\hookrightarrow I
. By assumption
I\in\mathcal{X}
, hence

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(g,\ I):\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(L_{1},\ I)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N\otimes_{B}L_{2},\ I)
is an epimorphism, by
Claim 1
. Hence there is an
A
-map
v:L_{1}\longrightarrow I
such that
vg=i.

Thus,
g
is a monomorphism.

Similar as
Claim 1
, one has

Claim 3:

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(f,\ Y):\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(L_{2},\ Y)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M\otimes_{A}L_{1},\ Y)
is an epimorphism, for any module
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
.

Similar as
Claim 2
, one has

Claim 4:

f
is a monomorphism.

We omit the similar proof of
Claim 3
and
Claim 4
.

Now, since
g
and
f
are monic, by Lemma
3.10
(1) and (2) one has

\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(\operatorname{Coker}g,\ \mathcal{X})=\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}({\rm C}_{A}L,\ \mathcal{X})\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X}))=0,

\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(\operatorname{Coker}f,\ \mathcal{Y})=\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}({\rm C}_{B}L,\ \mathcal{Y})\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(L,\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))=0

By definition,
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in\Delta({}^{\perp}\mathcal{X},\ {}^{\perp}\mathcal{Y})
. This completes the proof of
(1)
.

(2) This can be similarly proved, however, it is difficult to say that it is the dual of (1), thus we include a justification. It will be much convenient to use the second expression of a

\Lambda
-module.

Let
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\in\nabla(\mathcal{X}^{\perp},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\perp})
, i.e.,
\widetilde{f}
and
\widetilde{g}
are epimorphisms, and
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{X}^{\perp}
and
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{Y}^{\perp}
.
Since
\widetilde{f}
is an epimorphism and
\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(\mathcal{X},\ {\rm K}_{A}L)=\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(\mathcal{X},\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f})=0
, by Lemma
3.10
(3),
L\in{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})^{\perp}
.
Similarly, since
\widetilde{g}
is an epimorphism and
\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(\mathcal{Y},\ {\rm K}_{B}L)=\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(\mathcal{Y},\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g})=0
, by Lemma
3.10
(4),

L\in{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})^{\perp}
. Thus,
L\in{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})^{\perp}
.

Conversely, let
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\in\ {\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})^{\perp}
.

Claim 1:

\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(Y,\ \widetilde{g}):\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(Y,\ L_{2})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(Y,\ \operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1}))
is an epimorphism, for any module
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
.
In fact,
\forall\ u\in\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(Y,\ \operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1}))
,
consider
B
-map
\widetilde{g^{\prime}}:=(u,\widetilde{g}):Y\oplus L_{2}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ L_{1})
. Thus
g^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N\otimes_{B}(Y\oplus L_{2}),\ L_{1})
.
Put
\widetilde{f^{\prime}}=\binom{0}{\widetilde{f}}:L_{1}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,Y)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2}).
Thus
f^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M\otimes_{A}L_{1},\ Y\oplus L_{2})
.
Since
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
Y\oplus L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f^{\prime}},\widetilde{g^{\prime}}}
is indeed a
\Lambda
-module.

Then one has the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\xlongrightarrow{\binom{1}{\binom{0}{1}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
Y\oplus L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f^{\prime}},\widetilde{g^{\prime}}}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
(1,0)\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\longrightarrow 0.

(We stress that it is much convenient to use the second expression of

\Lambda
-modules. Otherwise, say, it is not direct to see that
{\binom{1}{\binom{0}{1}}}
is a
\Lambda
-map.)

Since
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}={\rm Z}_{B}Y\in{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
and
L\in\ {\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})^{\perp}\cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})^{\perp}
,
this exact sequence splits, i.e., there is a
\Lambda
-map

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha\\
(a,b)\end{smallmatrix}\right):\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
Y\oplus L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f^{\prime}},\widetilde{g^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}

such that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha\\
(a,b)\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
\binom{0}{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm Id}_{L}
.
So
\alpha=\operatorname{Id}_{L_{1}}
and
b={\rm Id}_{L_{2}}
.
This gives the commutative diagram

\textstyle{Y\oplus L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{g^{\prime}}=(u,\widetilde{g})}
\scriptstyle{(a,1)}
\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{g}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})}

commutes. Hence
u=\widetilde{g}a
. This proves
Claim 1
.

Claim 2:

\widetilde{g}
is an epimorphism. In fact, taking a
B
-epimorphism
q:Q\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})
with
Q
projective. Then
Q\in\mathcal{Y}
, hence

\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(Q,\ \widetilde{g}):\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(Q,\ L_{2})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(Q,\ \operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ L_{1}))
is an epimorphism. So there is a
B
-map
v:Q\longrightarrow L_{2}
with
q=\widetilde{g}v.
This proves
Claim 2
.

Similarly,
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(X,\ \widetilde{f}):\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(X,\ L_{1})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(X,\ \operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2}))
is an epimorphism for any
X\in\mathcal{X}
; and

\widetilde{f}
is an epimorphism.

It follows from Lemma
3.10
(3) and (4) that

\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(\mathcal{X},\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f})=\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(\mathcal{X},\ {\rm K}_{A}L)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X}),\ L)=0

and that

\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(\mathcal{Y},\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g})=\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(\mathcal{Y},\ {\rm K}_{B}L)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}({\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ L)=0.

By definition,
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\in\nabla(\mathcal{X}^{\perp},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\perp})
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.

(1)
Assume that
M_{A}
and
N_{B}
are flat modules.
Then
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
is closed under the kernels of epimorphisms if and only if
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
are closed under the kernels of epimorphisms.

(2)
Assume that
{}_{B}M
and
{}_{A}N
are projective.
Then
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
is closed under the cokernels of monomorphisms if and only if
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are closed under the cokernels of monomorphisms.

Proof.

(1)
Assume that
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
are closed under the kernels of epimorphisms.
Let
0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}M_{1}\\
M_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N_{1}\\
N_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{s,t}\longrightarrow 0
be an exact sequence with
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}M_{1}\\
M_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v},\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}N_{1}\\
N_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{s,t}\in\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
.
Thus
u,v,s,t
are monomorphisms,
\operatorname{Coker}u\in\mathcal{V},\ \operatorname{Coker}v\in\mathcal{U},\ \operatorname{Coker}s\in\mathcal{V}
, and
\operatorname{Coker}t\in\mathcal{U}.

Since
M_{A}
is flat, one has the commutative diagram with exact rows:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\alpha}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}M_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}N_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since
1\otimes\alpha
and
u
are monomorphisms, so is
f
. By Snake Lemma and the assumption
that
\mathcal{V}
is closed under the kernels of epimorphisms,
one knows that
\operatorname{Coker}f\in\mathcal{V}
. Similarly,
g
is a monomorphism and
\operatorname{Coker}g\in\ \mathcal{U}
. By definition
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
.
This proves that
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
is closed under the kernels of epimorphisms.

Conversely, using functors
{\rm T}_{A}
and
{\rm T}_{B}
, one sees that

\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
are closed under the kernels of epimorphisms.

(2)
Assume that
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are closed under the cokernels of monomorphisms. Let
0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}M_{1}\\
M_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N_{1}\\
N_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{s,t}\longrightarrow 0

be an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules with
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}M_{1}\\
M_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{u,v}\in\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
.
Thus
\widetilde{f},\ \widetilde{g},\ \widetilde{u},\ \widetilde{v}
are epimorphisms,
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{X},\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{Y},\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{u}\in\mathcal{X}
, and
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{v}\in\mathcal{Y}.

Since
{}_{B}M
is projective, one has the commutative diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}}
\textstyle{M_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{u}}
\textstyle{N_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{s}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,M_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(M,\beta)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,N_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since
\widetilde{u}
and
(M,\beta)
are epimorphisms, so is
\widetilde{s}
. By Snake Lemma and the assumption that
\mathcal{X}
is closed under taking the cokernels of monomorphisms,
one knows that
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{s}\in\mathcal{X}
. Similarly,
\widetilde{t}
is an epimorphism and
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{Y}
. By definition
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N_{1}\\
N_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{s,t}\in\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
. This proves that
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
is closed under the cokernels of monomorphisms.

Conversely, using functors
{\rm H}_{A}
and
{\rm H}_{B}
, one sees that

\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are closed under the cokernels of monomorphisms.
∎

3.7.
Proof of Theorem
3.2

(1)
It suffices to prove
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})=\ ^{\perp}(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\perp}).

In fact,
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})=\Delta(^{\perp}\mathcal{X},\ ^{\perp}\mathcal{Y})
.
Since
\mathcal{X}
contains all the injective
A
-modules and
\mathcal{Y}
contains all the injective
B
-modules, it follows from
Lemma
3.11
(1) that

\Delta(^{\perp}\mathcal{X},\ ^{\perp}\mathcal{Y})=\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})=\ ^{\perp}({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cup\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})).

Thus

{}^{\perp}(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\perp})

\displaystyle=\ ^{\perp}(\Delta(^{\perp}\mathcal{X},\ ^{\perp}\mathcal{Y})^{\perp})=\ ^{\perp}\{[^{\perp}({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cup\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))]^{\perp}\}

\displaystyle=\ ^{\perp}({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cup\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))=\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}).

By Lemma
3.12
(1),
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
is closed under the kernels of epimorphisms if and only if

\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
are closed under the kernels of epimorphisms.
That is,
(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\perp})
is hereditary if and only if
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
are hereditary.

(2)
Similarly, it suffices to show
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})=\ (^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))^{\perp}.

In fact,
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})=\nabla(\mathcal{U}^{\perp},\ \mathcal{V}^{\perp})
.
Since
\mathcal{U}
contains all the projective
A
-modules and
\mathcal{V}
contains all the projective
B
-modules, it follows from
Lemma
3.11
(2) that

\nabla(\mathcal{U}^{\perp},\ \mathcal{V}^{\perp})=\ {\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V})^{\perp}=({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\ \cup\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V}))^{\perp}.

Thus

\displaystyle(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))^{\perp}

\displaystyle=(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{U}^{\perp},\ \mathcal{V}^{\perp}))^{\perp}=\{^{\perp}[({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\ \cup\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V}))^{\perp}]\}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\ \cup\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V}))^{\perp}=\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}).

By Lemma
3.12
(2),
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
is
closed under the cokernels of monomorphisms if and only if
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are closed under the cokernels of monomorphisms. That is,
(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))
is hereditary if and only if
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
are hereditary.
\square

3.8.
Proof of Theorem
3.5

(1)
By Theorem
3.1
(1), one has cotorsion pair
({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
;
and by Theorem
3.2
(1), one has cotorsion pair
(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\perp})
. We will prove
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot}\subseteq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right).
By Lemma
3.11
(1) one has

\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot}=[\Delta({}^{\perp}\mathcal{X},\ {}^{\perp}\mathcal{Y})]^{\bot}=[^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})]^{\bot}.

Since by assumption
\operatorname{Tor}^{A}_{1}(M,\ \mathcal{U})=0=\operatorname{Tor}^{B}_{1}(N,\ \mathcal{V})
, it follows from Lemma
3.9
(1) that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\perp}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})^{\perp}=({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\ \cup\ {\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}))^{\perp}.

Thus, to show
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot}\subseteq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),
it suffices to show

{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\ \cup\ {\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})\subseteq\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}).

In fact, since
N\otimes_{B}M=0
, the structure map
g=0
of any
\Lambda
-module in
{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})
is a monomorphism,
it follows from Lemma
3.10
(1) that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}),\ {\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}({\rm C}_{A}{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}),\ \mathcal{X})=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})=0.

By Lemma
3.10
(2) one has

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}),\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}({\rm C}_{B}{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}),\ \mathcal{Y})=0

since
{\rm C}_{B}{\rm T}_{A}=0
.
So
{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\subseteq\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
.

Similarly, by Lemma
3.10
(1) one has

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}({\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ {\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}({\rm C}_{A}{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \mathcal{X})=0

since
{\rm C}_{A}{\rm T}_{B}=0.
Since
M\otimes_{A}N=0
, the structure map
f=0
of any
\Lambda
-module in
{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})
is a monomorphism,
it follows from Lemma
3.10
(2) that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}({\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}({\rm C}_{B}{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \mathcal{Y})=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})=0.

So
{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})\subseteq\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cap\ ^{\perp}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
. This completes the proof of (1).

(2) Comparing cotorsion pair
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
in Theorem
3.1
(2)
with
(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))
in Theorem
3.2
(2), we will prove
{}^{\bot}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})\subseteq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right).
This can be similarly done as (1). For convenience we include a brief justification. By Lemma
3.11
(2) one has

{}^{\bot}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})=\ ^{\bot}\nabla(\mathcal{U}^{\perp},\ \mathcal{V}^{\perp})=\ ^{\bot}[{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V})^{\perp}].

By Lemma
3.9
(2), one has

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}^{\bot}\mathcal{X}\\
{}^{\bot}\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\ ^{\bot}[{\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cup\ {\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})].

So, it suffices to show
{\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\ \cup\ {\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})\subseteq{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V})^{\perp}.

In fact, since
\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(N,\ \mathcal{X})=0
, it follows from Lemma
3.8
(3) that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U}),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}({\rm U}_{A}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U}),\ \mathcal{X})=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})=0

and

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}({\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}({\rm U}_{A}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \mathcal{X})=0.

Thus
{\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V})^{\perp}
.

Since
\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(M,\ \mathcal{Y})=0
, it follows from Lemma
3.8
(4) that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}({\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U}),\ {\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}({\rm U}_{B}{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U}),\ \mathcal{Y})=0

and

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}({\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ {\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}({\rm U}_{B}{\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \mathcal{Y})=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})=0,

which show
{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})\subseteq{\rm Z}_{A}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp}\ \cap\ {\rm Z}_{B}(\mathcal{V})^{\perp}
.
This completes the proof.
\square

4.
Identifications

We will prove that the four constructions of cotorsion pairs, given in Theorem
3.1
and Theorem
3.2
,
are pairwise generally different; and on the other hand, study the problem of identifications, i.e.,
we will show that, in many important cases, the cotorsion pairs in Series I coincide with the corresponding ones in Series II, and then we will get only two cotorsion pairs

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\ \ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})).

Since the both cotorsion pairs are explicitly given,
they can be used in finding Hovey triples, i.e., the abelian model structures on Morita rings.

4.1.
Generally different cotorsion pairs

For use in Section 6, we introduce the following notion.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\Omega
be a class of Morita rings,
(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
cotorsion pairs defined in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, for arbitrary
Morita rings
\Lambda\in\Omega
.
We say that
(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
are generally different, provided that there exist
\Lambda\in\Omega
, such that
(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})\neq(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Example 4.2
.

Generally different cotorsion pairs could be the same for some special Morita rings, as the following example shows.

Let
\Omega=\{\mbox{Morita ring}\ \Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)\ |\ \phi=0=\psi,\ _{B}N\ \mbox{and}\ _{A}M\ \mbox{are projective}\}.
Then

(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod})
and
(\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\perp})
are cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
,
\forall\ \Lambda\in\Omega
. See Theorem
3.1
(2).

If
M\neq 0
, then
\binom{A}{0}_{0,0}\notin\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}
. Thus
{}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}\neq\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}
for

\Lambda\in\Omega
with
M\neq 0
. Hence

(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod})
and
(\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\perp})
are generally different cotorsion pairs.
But they are the same for
\Lambda\in\Omega
with
M=0=N
.

4.2.
The four cotorsion pairs are pairwise generally different

By Theorems
3.1
and
3.2
, the cotorsion pairs

(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)),\ \ \ \ (\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\perp})

and

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}),\ \ \ \ (^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))

are defined in
\Lambda
-Mod,
\forall\ \Lambda\in\Omega
, where

\Omega=\{\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&A\end{smallmatrix}\right)\ |\ M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M,\ M_{A}\ \mbox{and}\ N_{B}\ \mbox{are flat},\ _{B}M\ \mbox{and}\ _{A}N\ \mbox{are projective}\}.

We will show that the four cotorsion pairs are pairwise generally different. For convenience, we will call the cotorsion pairs above the first, the second, the third, and the fourth cotorsion pairs.

Example 4.3
.

Let
A=B
be the path algebra
k(1\longrightarrow 2)
, where
{\rm char}\ k\neq 2
. Write the conjunction of paths from right to left. Thus
e_{1}Ae_{2}=0
and
e_{2}Ae_{1}\cong k
. Take
M=N=Ae_{2}\otimes_{k}e_{1}A
. Then
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{A}M
. Let
\Lambda
be the Morita ring
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&A\end{smallmatrix}\right).
Then
\Lambda\in\Omega.

Note that
{}_{A}M=\ _{A}N
is isomorphic to the simple projective left
A
-module
Ae_{2}=S_{2}
, and that
M_{A}=N_{A}
is isomorphic to the simple projective right
A
-module
e_{1}A
. Then
M\otimes_{A}Ae_{1}\cong Ae_{2}\otimes_{k}(e_{1}A\otimes_{A}Ae_{1})\cong S_{2}
. To see the left
A
-module structure on
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(M,Ae_{1})
, note that

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(M,Ae_{1})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(Ae_{2},Ae_{1})\cong e_{2}Ae_{1}\cong k
as
k
-spaces. For
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(M,Ae_{1})
given by
f(e_{2}\otimes_{k}e_{1})=e_{1}
, one has
e_{1}f=f
. Thus
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(M,Ae_{1})\cong S_{1}
as left
A
-modules. The Auslander-Reiten quiver of
A
is

\textstyle{Ae_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{S_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{S_{1}}

Take
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})=(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{A}\mathcal{I})=(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
. Note that
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}\nsubseteq\mathcal{Y},\ N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}.

Take
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}
.
Then
L\in{\rm Mon(\Lambda)}=\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})=\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
and
L\in\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Consider the exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{1}{0}\\
\binom{1}{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\oplus Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\oplus Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma&\sigma\\
0&\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma&\sigma\\
0&\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(0,1)\\
(0,1)\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}\longrightarrow 0.

This exact sequence does not split. In fact, if it splits, then
there is a
\Lambda
-map
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(a,b)\\
(c,d)\end{smallmatrix}\right):\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\oplus Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\oplus Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma&\sigma\\
0&\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma&\sigma\\
0&\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}
such that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(a,b)\\
(c,d)\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{1}{0}\\
\binom{1}{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right),

i.e.,
a=1=c.
Since the following diagrams

\textstyle{S_{2}\oplus S_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma&\sigma\\
0&\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{(1,b)}
\textstyle{S_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{Ae_{1}\oplus Ae_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1,d)}
\textstyle{Ae_{1}}

\textstyle{S_{2}\oplus S_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma&\sigma\\
0&\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{(1,d)}
\textstyle{S_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{Ae_{1}\oplus Ae_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1,b)}
\textstyle{Ae_{1}}

commute,
d+1=b
and
b+1=d
, which is a contradiction, since
{\rm char}\ k\neq 2
.

Thus
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(L,L)\neq 0
. This means
L\notin\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Since
L\in{\rm Mon(\Lambda)}
,

{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq{\rm Mon(\Lambda)}
.
Thus, the first cotorsion pair
is not equal to the second one, i.e.,

(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))=({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})=(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\perp}).

Since
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
,
it follows that
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})=(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}).

Since
L\notin\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, the first cotorsion pair
is not equal to the third one:

(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))=({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I})=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

Since
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\notin{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)=\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
, the second cotorsion pair
is not equal to the third one:

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\perp})\neq(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I})=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

By definition
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})=\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{A}\mathcal{I})=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}
. Thus

(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))=(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I})
, i.e., the fourth cotorsion pair is exactly
third cotorsion pair. Therefore, the first cotorsion pair is not equal to the fourth one, and the second cotorsion pair
is not equal to the fourth one.

Finally, to see the third cotorsion pair is not equal to the fourth one, namely,

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\neq(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))

we take
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})=(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})=(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
.
Note that
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ \mathcal{Y})\nsubseteq\mathcal{U},\ \operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \mathcal{X})\nsubseteq\mathcal{V}.

Take
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}
as above.
Then
L\in\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Since
\widetilde{\sigma}:Ae_{1}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(M,Ae_{1})\cong S_{1}
is exactly the epimorphism
\pi:Ae_{1}\longrightarrow S_{1}
, by definition

L\in{\rm Epi(\Lambda)}=\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})=\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
.
Since
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(L,L)\neq 0
,
L\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}
. Thus

\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}
, and hence the third cotorsion pair is not equal to the fourth one:

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\neq(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})).

All together, we have proved that the four cotorsion pairs are pairwise generally different.
In fact, we have found an example
\Lambda
, such that the four constructions of cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda
-Mod
are pairwise different.

4.3.
Main results on identification

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
,
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
be cotorsion pairs in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
and in
B
-Mod
, respectively.

(1)
Assume that
\operatorname{Tor}^{A}_{1}(M,\ \mathcal{U})=0=\operatorname{Tor}^{B}_{1}(N,\ \mathcal{V})
. If

M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
or
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, then

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot})=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

Thus
(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Moreover, if
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
and
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, then

\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}).

(2)
Assume that
\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(M,\ \mathcal{Y})=0=\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(N,\ \mathcal{X})
. If
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ \mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
or
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
, then

(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

Thus
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Moreover, if
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ \mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
, then

\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}).

4.4.
Applications

In Theorem
4.4
, taking one of
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and

(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
being the projective cotorsion pair or the injective cotorsion pair,
and another being an arbitrary cotorsion pair, one has

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.

(1)
If
N_{B}
is flat, then for any
cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
in
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}

one has

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot})=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

Thus
(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Moreover, if
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}

(
e.g., this is the case if
{}_{B}M
is injective
)
,
then

\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V})={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}).

(2)
If
M_{A}
is flat, then for any
cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
one has

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot})=({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

Thus
(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\text{\rm-Mod}})
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Moreover, if
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{X}

(
e.g., this is the case if
{}_{A}N
is injective
)
, then

\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}).

(3)
If
{}_{B}M
is projective, then for any cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
in
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
one has

(^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y}))=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

Thus
(\binom{A\text{\rm-Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{V}},\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y}))
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Moreover, if
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}

(
e.g., this is the case if
B
is quasi-Frobenius and
N_{B}
is flat
)
, then

\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y})={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}).

(4)
If
{}_{A}N
is projective, then for any cotorsion pair

(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
one has

(^{\bot}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

Thus
(\binom{\mathcal{U}}{B\text{\rm-Mod}},\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Moreover, if
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{U}

(
e.g., this is the case if
A
is quasi-Frobenius and
M_{A}
is flat
)
, then
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}).

Proof.

(1) Taking
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})=(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
in Theorem
4.4
(1). Then

N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}=\mathcal{X}
. By Theorem
4.4
(1) one has

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot})=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

If
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
, i.e.,

M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}=M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
,
then by Theorem
4.4
(1),

\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V})={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}).

Assume that
{}_{B}M
is injective. For any
P\in\ _{A}\mathcal{P}
,
as a left
B
-module,
M\otimes_{A}P
is a direct summand of
a direct sum of copies of
{}_{B}M
. Since
{}_{B}M
is injective and
B
is left noetherian (note that
\Lambda
is assumed to be an Artin algebra, hence
B
is an Artin algebra),

M\otimes_{A}P
is an injective left
B
-module, and hence it is in
\mathcal{Y}
. Thus

M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
, and hence

\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V})={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),
by Theorem
4.4
(1).

(2) Taking
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})=(_{B}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
in Theorem
4.4
(1). Then

M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}\subseteq B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}=\mathcal{Y}
. By Theorem
4.4
(1) one has

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot})=({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

If
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, then by Theorem
4.4
(1),

\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}).

Assume that
A
is
{}_{A}N
is injective. Then

N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\ _{A}\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{X},
and hence

\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}).

(3) Taking
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})=(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{A}\mathcal{I})
in Theorem
4.4
(2). Then

\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ \mathcal{Y})\subseteq A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}=\mathcal{U}.

By Theorem
4.4
(2) one has

(^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y}))=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

If
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
,
then by Theorem
4.4
(2),
\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y})={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
.

Assume that
B
is quasi-Frobenius and
N_{B}
is flat. Then

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\ _{B}\mathcal{I}=\ _{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
, and thus

\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y})={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
, by Theorem
4.4
(2).

(4) Taking
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})=(B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})
in Theorem
4.4
(2). Then

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \mathcal{X})\subseteq B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}=\mathcal{V}.

By Theorem
4.4
(2) one has

(^{\bot}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

If
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
,
then by Theorem
4.4
(2),
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I})
.

Assume that
A
is quasi-Frobenius and
M_{A}
is flat. Then
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\ _{A}\mathcal{I}=\ _{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{U},

and hence
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I})
.
∎

4.5.
Proof of Theorem
4.4

(1) By Theorem
3.5
(1), one has cotorsion pairs

(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)),\ \ \ \ \ (\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\perp})

with
{}^{\bot}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\subseteq\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
.
To see that they are equal, it remains to prove
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})\subseteq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

Let
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
. By definition there are exact sequences

0\rightarrow M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{f}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{p_{1}}\operatorname{Coker}f\rightarrow 0\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ 0\rightarrow N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{g}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{p_{2}}\operatorname{Coker}g\rightarrow 0

with
\operatorname{Coker}f\in\ \mathcal{V}
and
\operatorname{Coker}g\in\ \mathcal{U}
. Since
N\otimes_{B}M=0=M\otimes_{A}N
, it follows that

1\otimes p_{1}:N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\longrightarrow N\otimes_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\ \ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ 1\otimes p_{2}:M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\longrightarrow M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g

are isomorphisms.

Case I:
Assume that
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
. Then

M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\cong M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\in M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}.

Since
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is a cotorsion pair, the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{f}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{p_{1}}\operatorname{Coker}f\longrightarrow 0

splits. Thus there are
B
-maps
f^{\prime}:L_{2}\longrightarrow M\otimes_{A}L_{1}
and
\sigma_{1}:\operatorname{Coker}f\longrightarrow L_{2}

such that

f^{\prime}f=1_{M\otimes_{A}L_{1}},\ \ \ p_{1}\sigma_{1}=1_{\operatorname{Coker}f},\ \ \ ff^{\prime}+\sigma_{1}p_{1}=1_{L_{2}},\ \ f^{\prime}\sigma_{1}=0.

Thus
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f^{\prime}\\
p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right):L_{2}\longrightarrow(M\otimes_{A}L_{1})\oplus\operatorname{Coker}f
is a
B
-isomorphism, and

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f^{\prime}\\
p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\end{smallmatrix}\right):\ L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\cong\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
(M\otimes_{A}L_{1})\oplus\operatorname{Coker}f\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\binom{1}{0},\ g(1\otimes p_{1})^{-1}}

is a
\Lambda
-isomorphism, and

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\\
\operatorname{Coker}f\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}g(1\otimes p_{1})^{-1}\\
\binom{0}{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
(M\otimes_{A}L_{1})\oplus\operatorname{Coker}f\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\binom{1}{0},\ g(1\otimes p_{1})^{-1}}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}\\
(1\otimes p_{2},0)\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Coker}g\\
M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\rightarrow 0

is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules, i.e.,

0\longrightarrow{\rm T}_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
(M\otimes_{A}L_{1})\oplus\operatorname{Coker}f\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\binom{1}{0},\ g(1\otimes p_{1})^{-1}}\longrightarrow{\rm T}_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\longrightarrow 0

is exact.

Since
\operatorname{Coker}f\in\ \mathcal{V}
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is a cotorsion pair,
by Lemma
3.8
(2),
{\rm T}_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Since
\operatorname{Coker}g\in\mathcal{U}
and
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is a cotorsion pair, by Lemma
3.8
(1),
{\rm T}_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Thus
L\cong\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
(M\otimes_{A}L_{1})\oplus\operatorname{Coker}f\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\binom{1}{0},\ g(1\otimes p_{1})^{-1}}\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Case II:
Assume that
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
. This is similar to
Case I
.
We include the main steps. Since
N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\cong N\otimes_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\in N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X},

the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{g}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{p_{2}}\operatorname{Coker}g\longrightarrow 0

splits. Then
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\cong\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N\otimes_{B}L_{2})\oplus\operatorname{Coker}g\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f(1\otimes p_{2})^{-1},\binom{1}{0}}

and

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Coker}g\\
M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{0}{1}\\
f(1\otimes p_{2})^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N\otimes_{B}L_{2})\oplus\operatorname{Coker}g\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f(1\otimes p_{2})^{-1},\binom{1}{0}}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(1\otimes p_{1},0)\\
p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\\
\operatorname{Coker}f\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\rightarrow 0

is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules, i.e.,

0\longrightarrow{\rm T}_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N\otimes_{B}L_{2})\oplus\operatorname{Coker}g\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f(1\otimes p_{2})^{-1},\binom{1}{0}}\longrightarrow{\rm T}_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\longrightarrow 0

is exact.
By Lemma
3.8
(1),
{\rm T}_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}}
; and
by Lemma
3.8
(2),
{\rm T}_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{Y}}{\mathcal{Y}}.

Thus
L\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{Y}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{Y}}
.

Finally, assume that
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
and
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
.
Then from the proof above one sees that both
0\rightarrow M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{f}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{p_{1}}\operatorname{Coker}f\rightarrow 0
and

0\rightarrow N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{g}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{p_{2}}\operatorname{Coker}g\rightarrow 0
split, and

L\cong\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Coker}g\\
M\otimes_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f\\
\operatorname{Coker}f\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}={\rm T}_{A}\operatorname{Coker}g\oplus{\rm T}_{B}\operatorname{Coker}f.

Thus
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})\subseteq{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}).
The inclusion

{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})\subseteq\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
is clear. Thus shows

\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}).

(2) By Theorem
3.5
(2), one has cotorsion pairs

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}),\ \ \ \ \ (^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))

with
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\bot}\subseteq\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
.
To see that they are equal, it remains to prove
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})\subseteq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\bot}
.

Here it is much more convenient to use the second expression of
\Lambda
-modules. Thus, let
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\in\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
, where
\widetilde{f}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(X,\ \operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,Y))
and

\widetilde{g}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(Y,\ \operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,X))
. By definition there are exact sequences

0\rightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\xlongrightarrow{i_{1}}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})\rightarrow 0\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ 0\rightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\xlongrightarrow{i_{2}}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{\widetilde{g}}\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\rightarrow 0

with
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\in\ \mathcal{X}
and
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\in\ \mathcal{Y}
. Since
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, it follows that

(N,\ i_{1}):\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ (M,\ i_{2}):\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2}).

Case I:
Assume that
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
. Then

\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ L_{2})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g})\in\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}.

Since
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is a cotorsion pair, the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\xlongrightarrow{i_{1}}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})\longrightarrow 0

splits. Thus there are
A
-maps
\alpha:\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ L_{2})\longrightarrow L_{1}
and
\pi_{1}:L_{1}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}

such that

\pi_{1}i_{1}=1_{\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}},\ \ \ \widetilde{f}\alpha=1_{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})},\ \ \ \alpha\widetilde{f}+i_{1}\pi_{1}=1_{L_{1}},\ \ \ \pi_{1}\alpha=0.

Hence
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi_{1}\\
\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right):L_{1}\cong\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})
and

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{\pi_{1}}{\widetilde{f}}\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right):\ L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\cong\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ L_{2})\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{(0,\ 1),\ \binom{(N,\pi_{1})\widetilde{g}}{0}}.

Moreover,

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(M,\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g})\\
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\xrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{0}{(M,i_{2})}\\
i_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\oplus(M,\ L_{2})\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{(0,1),\ \binom{(N,\pi_{1})\widetilde{g}}{0}}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(1,0)\\
(N,\pi_{1})\widetilde{g}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\\
(N,\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f})\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\rightarrow 0

is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules, i.e.,

0\longrightarrow{\rm H}_{B}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ L_{2})\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{(0,\ 1),\ \binom{(N,\pi_{1})\widetilde{g}}{0}}\longrightarrow{\rm H}_{A}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\longrightarrow 0

is exact. (We stress that all the
\Lambda
-modules are in the second expression.)

Since
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\in\ \mathcal{Y}
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is a cotorsion pair,
by Lemma
3.8
(4),
{\rm H}_{B}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\in\binom{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\bot}
.
Since
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{X}
and
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is a cotorsion pair, by Lemma
3.8
(3),
{\rm H}_{A}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\in\binom{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}
.
Thus
L\cong\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{(0,\ 1),\ \binom{(N,\pi_{1})\widetilde{g}}{0}}\in\binom{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}
.

Case II:
Assume that
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
. This is similar to
Case I
.
Since
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ L_{1})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f})\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V},

the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\xlongrightarrow{i_{2}}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{\widetilde{g}}\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\longrightarrow 0

splits. Thus
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\cong\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\binom{(M,i_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{f}}{0},(0,1)}

and

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\\
(N,\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f})\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}i_{1}\\
\binom{0}{(N,i_{1})}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\oplus(N,L_{1})\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\binom{(M,i_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{f}}{0},(0,1)}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(M,i_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{f}\\
(1,0)\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(M,\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g})\\
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\rightarrow 0

is exact, i.e.,

0\longrightarrow{\rm H}_{A}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\binom{(M,i_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{f}}{0},(0,1)}\longrightarrow{\rm H}_{B}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\longrightarrow 0

is exact. By Lemma
3.8
(3),
{\rm H}_{A}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\in\binom{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}
; and by Lemma
3.8
(4),
{\rm H}_{B}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\in\binom{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}.

Thus
L\in\binom{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}
.

Finally, assume that
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ \mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
. Then both

0\rightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\xlongrightarrow{i_{1}}L_{1}\xlongrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})\rightarrow 0
and
0\rightarrow\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\xlongrightarrow{i_{2}}L_{2}\xlongrightarrow{\widetilde{g}}\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\rightarrow 0
splits, and

L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\cong\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f})\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g})\\
\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}={\rm H}_{A}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{f}\oplus{\rm H}_{B}\operatorname{Ker}\widetilde{g}\in{\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}).

Conversely, it is clear that

{\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
.
Thus
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
.

\square

4.6.
Remark

In Theorem
3.5
, taking one of
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and

(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
being the projective cotorsion pair or the injective cotorsion pair,
and another being an arbitrary cotorsion pair, we conclude as follows: where “
=
” follows from Corollary
4.5
, and
“
\neq
” follows from Example
4.3
.

(1)
If
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})=(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
, and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})

is an arbitrary cotorsion pair in
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, then

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot})=({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

But, in general
(^{\bot}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ \mathcal{Y}))\neq(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

(2)
If
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})=(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{A}\mathcal{I})
, and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
an arbitrary cotorsion pair in
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
,
then in general
(\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot})\neq({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

However, one has

(^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y}))=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

(3)
If
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is an arbitrary cotorsion pair in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, and

(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})=(_{B}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
, then

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot})=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

But, in general
(^{\bot}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))\neq(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

(4)
If
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is an arbitrary cotorsion pair in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, and

(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})=(B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})
, then in general

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\bot})\neq({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

However, one has

(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}).

The above information is listed in Table 1 below, where

\mathcal{A}:=A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ \ \ \ \mathcal{B}:=B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ \ \ \ \mbox{proj.}:=\mbox{projective}.
