4.7.
Monomorphism categories and epimorphism categories

Even if in the case of “
\neq
” in general, the two cotorsion pairs can be the same, in some special cases.

If
{}_{A}N
and
{}_{B}M
are projective, then cotorsion pairs
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
and
(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))=(^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))

are not equal in general (cf. Example
4.3
); but the following result claims that they can be the same in some special cases.

Also, if
M_{A}
and
N_{B}
are flat, then
({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})
in general
(cf. Example
4.3
); but the following result claims that they can be the same in some special cases.

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring which is an Artin algebra with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.
Assume that
A
and
B
are quasi-Frobenius rings, that
{}_{A}N
and
{}_{B}M
are projective, and that
M_{A}
and
N_{B}
are flat. Then

{\rm(1)}

\Lambda
is a Gorenstein ring with
{\rm inj.dim}_{\Lambda}\Lambda\leq 1
, and

{}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}=\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}=\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}^{\leq 1}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}^{<\infty}.

{\rm(2)}
The cotorsion pair
(^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}})
coincides with
({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot});
and it is
exactly the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair
({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1})
. So, it is complete and hereditary, and

{\rm GP}(\Lambda)={\rm Mon}(\Lambda)={}^{\perp}\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \ \ \ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}.

{\rm(2)^{\prime}}
The cotorsion pair
(\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\perp})
coincides with
(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda));
and it
is exactly the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))
. So, it is complete and hereditary, and

{\rm GI}(\Lambda)={\rm Epi}(\Lambda)=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}{}^{\perp},\ \ \ \ ^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}.

Example 4.7
.

(1)

We give an example to justify the existence of the assumptions in
Theorem
4.6
.
Let
Q
be the quiver

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{2\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{3\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{n\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

and
A=kQ/J^{h}
, where
J
is the ideal of path algebra
kQ
generated by all the arrows, and
2\leq h\leq n
. Then
A
is a self-injective algebra, in particular, a quasi-Frobenius ring. Let
e=e_{i}
,
e^{\prime}=e_{j}
, where
1\leq i<j\leq n
, satisfying
j-i\geq h
. Then
e^{\prime}Ae=e_{j}Ae_{i}=0
.
Put
M:=Ae\otimes_{k}e^{\prime}A
. Then
{}_{A}M
and
M_{A}
are projective, and
M\otimes_{A}M=(Ae\otimes_{k}e^{\prime}A)\otimes_{A}(Ae\otimes_{k}e^{\prime}A)=Ae\otimes_{k}(e^{\prime}A\otimes_{A}Ae)\otimes_{k}e^{\prime}A=0.

Take
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&M\\
M&A\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Then
\Lambda
satisfies
all the conditions in
Theorem
4.6
.

Remark 4.8
.

(1)

Non-zero Morita rings
\Lambda
in
Theorem
4.6
do not satisfy the sufficient condition for self-injective algebras in
[GrP, Proposition 3.7]
. In fact,

\Lambda
can not be quasi-Frobenius
:
otherwise
{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)={\rm GP}(\Lambda)=\Lambda
-
Mod
, which is absurd
!

(2)
Although
Theorem
4.6
does not give new cotorsion pairs, in the sense that
they are just the Gorenstein-projective
(
respectively, Gorenstein-injective
)
cotorsion pairs,
however,
{\rm GP}(\Lambda)={\rm Mon}(\Lambda)
is a new result. In the special case of triangular matrix rings, this is known, by

[LiZ, Thm. 1.1], [XZ, Cor.1.5], [Z2, Thm.1.4], [LuoZ1, Thm.4.1], [ECIT, Thm.3.5]
. For more
relations between monomorphism categories and the Gorenstein-projective modules, we refer to
[Z1], [GrP], [LuoZ2], [GaP], [ZX], [HLXZ]
.

4.8.
Modules
\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}
and
\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}

To prove Theorem
4.6
, we need the following fact, which is of independent interest.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M.

(1)
Assume that
{}_{A}N
and
{}_{B}M
are projective modules. Let
\binom{P}{Q}_{f,g}\in\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}
. Then

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Q\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-\binom{g}{1}\\
\binom{1}{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Q\\
Q\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{1}{f},&\binom{g}{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
Q\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\rightarrow 0

is a projective resolution of
\binom{P}{Q}_{f,g}
. In particular,
{\rm proj.dim}\binom{P}{Q}_{f,g}\leq 1,\ \forall\ \binom{P}{Q}_{f,g}\in\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}
.

(2)
Assume that
M_{A}
and
N_{B}
are flat modules. Let
\binom{I}{J}_{f,g}\in\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}
. Then

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I\\
J\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{1}{\widetilde{g}}\\
\binom{\widetilde{f}}{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,\epsilon^{\prime}_{{}_{I}}}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
J\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\epsilon_{{}_{J}},0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{\widetilde{f}}{1},&-\binom{1}{\widetilde{g}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\rightarrow 0

is an injective resolution of
\binom{I}{J}_{f,g}
. In particular,
{\rm inj.dim}\binom{I}{J}_{f,g}\leq 1,\ \forall\ \binom{I}{J}_{f,g}\in\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}
.

Remark 4.10
.

The condition
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
can not be relaxed to
\phi=0=\psi
. Otherwise, for example in
(1)
,
\operatorname{Ker}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{1}{f},&\binom{g}{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Q\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{-(1_{M}\otimes_{A}g),-(1_{N}\otimes_{B}f)}
, which is no longer a projective left
\Lambda
-module.

The similar remark for (2).

Proof of Lemma
4.9
(1) Thanks to the assumption
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M,
the given maps are
\Lambda
-maps (otherwise
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-\binom{g}{1}\\
\binom{1}{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is not necessarily a
\Lambda
-map in general, even if
\phi=0=\psi
). We omit the details.
The given sequence of
\Lambda
-modules is exact, since

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\binom{-g}{1}}
\textstyle{P\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1,g)}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

and

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\binom{-1}{f}}
\textstyle{(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f,1)}
\textstyle{Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

are exact.

We claim that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Q\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}
is a projective left
\Lambda
-module.
In fact, since
{}_{A}N
and
{}_{B}Q
are projective,
N\otimes_{B}Q
is a projective left
A
-module. Since
M\otimes_{A}N=0
, it follows that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Q\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Q\\
M\otimes_{A}N\otimes_{B}Q\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}

is a projective left
\Lambda
-module. Similarly,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}
is a projective left
\Lambda
-module. Thus,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Q\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}=\binom{N\otimes_{B}Q}{0}_{0,0}\oplus\binom{0}{M\otimes_{A}P}_{0,0}
is a projective left
\Lambda
-module.

(2) This can be similarly proved as (1). Since
N\otimes_{B}M=0=M\otimes_{A}N,
the given sequence is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-maps.
Since
M_{A}
is flat and
{}_{B}J
is injective,
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)
is an injective left
A
-module, and hence

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J))\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}

is an injective left
\Lambda
-module. Similarly,
\binom{0}{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)}_{0,0}
is an injective left
\Lambda
-module.
Thus,
\binom{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)}{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)}_{0,0}=\binom{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)}{0}_{0,0}\oplus\binom{0}{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)}_{0,0}
is an injective left
\Lambda
-module.

\square

4.9.
Proof of Theorem
4.6
.

(1) Since
A
is quasi-Frobenius,
{}_{A}A\in\ _{A}\mathcal{I}
.
Since
B
is quasi-Frobenius and
{}_{B}M
is projective,
{}_{B}M\in\ _{B}\mathcal{I}
.
Since
M_{A}
and
N_{B}
are flat, it follows from Lemma
4.9
(2) that
{\rm inj.dim}_{\Lambda}\binom{A}{M}_{1,0}\leq 1
.

Similarly, since
A
is quasi-Frobenius and
{}_{A}N
is projective,
{}_{A}N\in\ _{A}\mathcal{I}
.
Since
B
is quasi-Frobenius,
{}_{B}B\in\ _{B}\mathcal{I}
.
Since
M_{A}
and
N_{B}
are flat,
{\rm inj.dim}_{\Lambda}\binom{N}{B}_{0,1}\leq 1,

by Lemma
4.9
(2).

Thus,
{\rm inj.dim}_{\Lambda}\Lambda\leq 1
.
By the right module version of Lemma
4.9
(2) one knows
{\rm inj.dim}\Lambda_{\Lambda}\leq 1.

Thus
\Lambda
is a Gorenstein ring.

Since
\Lambda
is Gorenstein with
{\rm inj.dim}_{\Lambda}\Lambda\leq 1
, it is well-known that
{}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}^{\leq 1}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}^{<\infty}.

Since
{}_{A}N
and
{}_{B}M
are projective modules, it follows from Lemma
4.9
(1) that
\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}\subseteq\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}
. On the other hand, for any
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},

let
0\longrightarrow\binom{P_{11}}{P_{12}}\longrightarrow\binom{P_{01}}{P_{02}}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\longrightarrow 0
be a projective resolution of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
. Then
one has exact sequence
0\longrightarrow P_{11}\longrightarrow P_{01}\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow 0
. Since
{}_{A}N
is projective,
P_{11}
and
P_{01}
are projective (cf. Subsection 2.5), and hence injective. Thus the exact sequence splits and hence
X
is projective. Similarly,
Y
is projective. This shows
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}
. Hence
\ {}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}=\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}=\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}
.

(2) By (1),
\Lambda
is Gorenstein and
\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
. Thus,
{}^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}={\rm GP}(\Lambda)

and
({}^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}})
is just the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair, so it is complete and hereditary.

By Theorem
3.5
(1),
{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot}\subseteq\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
.
Thus, to see
({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}),
it suffices to show
{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)\subseteq{\rm GP}(\Lambda)
.
Since
\Lambda
is Gorenstein,
{\rm GP}(\Lambda)=\ ^{\bot_{\geq 1}}\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}
. See Subsection 2.9.
While
{\rm inj.dim}_{\Lambda}\Lambda\leq 1
, each projective
\Lambda
-module is of injective dimension
\leq 1
.
It follows that
{}^{\bot}\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}=\ ^{\bot_{\geq 1}}\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}.

Thus, it suffices to show
{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)\subseteq\ ^{\bot}\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}
, namely,
it suffices to show

\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}))=0.

This is indeed true. In fact, let
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\in{\rm Mon}(\Lambda).
For any
P\in\ _{A}\mathcal{P}
, there is an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\xlongrightarrow{\binom{0}{1}}{\rm T}_{A}P=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\xlongrightarrow{\binom{1}{0}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\longrightarrow 0.

By Lemma
3.10
(2) one has

\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g},\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0})=\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(\operatorname{Coker}f,\ M\otimes_{A}P)=0

since
M\otimes_{A}P
is projective as a left
B
-module (and hence injective). By Lemma
3.10
(1), one has

\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g},\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0})=\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(\operatorname{Coker}g,\ P)=0.

Thus
\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g},\ {\rm T}_{A}P)=0
. This shows
\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P}))=0.

Similarly,
\operatorname{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}))=0.
Thus
({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}),

in particular,

{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)=\mathcal{GP}(\Lambda)=\ {}^{\perp}\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \ \ \ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}.

The assertion
{\rm(2)^{\prime}}
is the dual of (2).
\square

5.
Completeness

To study abelian model structures on Morita rings, a key step is to know the completeness of
cotorsion pairs in Morita rings.

5.1.
Completeness via cogenerations by sets

First, by [ET2, Theorem 10], one has

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
,
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})
cotorsion pairs in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
and
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, cogenerated by sets
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
, respectively.

(1)
If
\operatorname{Tor}^{A}_{1}(M,\mathcal{U})=0=\operatorname{Tor}^{B}_{1}(N,\mathcal{V})
, then cotorsion pair
(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}},\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}})
is cogenerated by
{\rm T}_{A}(S_{1})\cup{\rm T}_{B}(S_{2}),
and hence complete.

(2)
If
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then cotorsion pair
(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))
is cogenerated by
{\rm Z}_{A}(S_{1})\cup{\rm Z}_{B}(S_{2})
, and hence complete.

Proof.

(1) By Theorem
3.1
(1),
(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}},\ \binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}})
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda
-Mod. By Lemma
3.9
(1),

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}S_{1}^{\perp}\\
S_{2}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=({\rm T}_{A}(S_{1})\cup{\rm T}_{B}(S_{2}))^{\perp}.
Thus,

(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}},\ \binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}})
is complete, by Proposition
2.4
.

(2) Without loss of generality, one may assume that
S_{1}\supseteq\ _{A}\mathcal{P}
and

S_{2}\supseteq\ _{B}\mathcal{P}
. Then by Lemma
3.11
(2) one has
\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})=\nabla(S_{1}^{\perp},\ S_{2}^{\perp})=({\rm Z}_{A}(S_{1})\cup{\rm Z}_{B}(S_{2}))^{\perp}.

∎

Proposition
5.1
gives some information on the completeness of the cotorsion pairs in Morita rings.
However, since Proposition
2.4
has no dual versions in general, there are no results on the completeness of
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
and
(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V})
,
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})^{\bot})
; moreover, it is more natural to study the completeness of the cotorsion pairs given in
Theorems
3.1
and
3.2
, directly from the completeness of
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})
, rather than requiring that
they are cogenerated by sets.
Thus, we need module-theoretical methods to the completeness of the cotorsion pairs in Morita rings.

Such a general investigation is difficult.
We will deal with this question, by assuming that one of
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})

is arbitrary, and that another is the projective or injective cotorsion pair. In view of Section 4, we only consider cotorsion pairs in Theorem
3.1
.

5.2.
Main results on completeness

Take
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})
to be an arbitrary complete cotorsion pair in
B
-Mod.
For cotorsion pair
({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
in Theorem
3.1
(1),
taking
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})=(_{A}\mathcal{P},A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
, we have assertion (1) below;
for cotorsion pair
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
in Theorem
3.1
(2),
taking
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})=(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{A}\mathcal{I})
, we have assertion (2) below.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
,
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
a complete cotorsion pair in
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. Suppose that
N_{B}
is flat and
{}_{B}M
is projective.

(1)
If
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
,
then
({}^{\perp}\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{Y}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{Y}})
is a complete cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod};

and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is hereditary.

Moreover, if
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then
{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),
and hence

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a complete cotorsion pair
;
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is hereditary.

(2)
If
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
, then
(\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp})

is a complete cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod};
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is hereditary.

Moreover, if
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then
\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
, and hence

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))

is a complete cotorsion pair
;

and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is hereditary.

Remark 5.3
.

(1)
{}_{B}M
is injective, then
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
always
holds.

(2)

If
B
is quasi-Frobenius and
N_{B}
is flat, then
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
always holds.

Take
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})
to be an arbitrary complete cotorsion pair in
A
-Mod.
For cotorsion pair
({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
in Theorem
3.1
(1),
taking
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})=(_{B}\mathcal{P},B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
, we have assertion (1) below;
for cotorsion pair
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
in Theorem
3.1
(2),
taking
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})=(B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})
, we have assertion (2) below.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
, and
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
a complete cotorsion pair in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.
Suppose that
M_{A}
is flat and
{}_{A}N
is projective.

(1)
If
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
,
then
({}^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}})
is a complete cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod};
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is hereditary.

Moreover, if
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then
{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P})
, and hence

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a complete cotorsion pair
;
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is hereditary.

(2)
If
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
, then

(\binom{\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \binom{\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}^{\perp})
is a complete cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod};
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is hereditary.

Moreover, if
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I})
, and hence

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}))

is a complete cotorsion pair
;
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is hereditary.

Remark 5.5
.

(1)

If
{}_{A}N
is injective, then
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
always holds.

(2)
If
A
is quasi-Frobenius and
M_{A}
is flat, then
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
always holds.

5.3.
Lemmas for Theorem
5.2

To prove Theorem
5.2
(1), we need

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
.
Suppose
{}_{B}M
is projective. For a
\Lambda
-module
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
, let
\pi:P\longrightarrow L_{1}
be an epimorphism with
{}_{A}P
projective,
and
0\longrightarrow Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\longrightarrow}}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}L_{2}\longrightarrow 0
an exact sequence.
Then there is an exact sequence of the form
:

0\rightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}K\\
(M\otimes P)\oplus Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}}\longrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
M\otimes P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}}\oplus{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes V\\
V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}}\xlongrightarrow{(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi\\
f(1\otimes\pi)\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}g(1\otimes\pi^{\prime})\\
\pi^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))}{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}}\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

For convenience, rewrite the sequence as

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}K\\
(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\oplus(N\otimes_{B}V)\\
(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1_{M\otimes P}&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1_{N\otimes V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(\pi,\ g(1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime}))\\
(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\rightarrow 0.

We claim that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(\pi,g(1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime}))\\
(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is a
\Lambda
-epimorphism.
In fact, by
\phi=0=\psi
,
f(1_{M}\otimes g)=0=g(1_{N}\otimes f)
. Hence

f(1_{M}\otimes g(1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime}))=0:\ M\otimes_{A}N\otimes_{B}V\longrightarrow L_{2}

and

g(1_{N}\otimes f(1_{M}\otimes\pi))=0:\ N\otimes_{B}M\otimes P\longrightarrow L_{1}.

Thus, the following diagrams commute:

\textstyle{(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus(M\otimes_{A}N\otimes_{B}V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1_{M\otimes P}&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{(1_{M}\otimes\pi,1_{M}\otimes g(1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime}))}
\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})}
\textstyle{L_{2}}

\textstyle{(N\otimes_{B}M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus(N\otimes_{B}V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1_{N\otimes V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{(1_{N}\otimes f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime})}
\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{P\oplus(N\otimes_{B}V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\pi,\ g(1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime}))}
\textstyle{L_{1}}

i.e.,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(\pi,g(1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime})\\
(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is a
\Lambda
-map. Clearly, it is an epimorphism.

It remains to see that
\operatorname{Ker}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(\pi,g(1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime})\\
(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is of the form
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}K\\
(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}
.

In fact, as a
\Lambda
-module,
\operatorname{Ker}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(\pi,g(1_{N}\otimes\pi^{\prime})\\
(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is of the form

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}K\\
K^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}
,
where
K^{\prime}=\operatorname{Ker}(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})
.
Thus, it suffices to show
\operatorname{Ker}(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})\cong(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus Y.

Since
{}_{A}P
is projective,
M\otimes_{A}P
is a direct summand of copies of
{}_{B}M
, as a left
B
-module.
While by assumption
{}_{B}M
is projective, it follows that

M\otimes_{A}P
is a projective left
B
-module. Thus, there is a
B
-map
h
such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{1_{M}\otimes\pi}
\textstyle{M\otimes L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Then it is clear that

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
-h&\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi),\ \pi^{\prime})}
\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is exact. This completes the proof.
∎

To prove Theorem
5.2
(2), we need the following lemma, in which it is more convenient to write
a
\Lambda
-module in the second expression.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
. Suppose
N_{B}
is flat. For
\Lambda
-module
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}
, let
\sigma:L_{1}\longrightarrow I
be a monomorphism with
{}_{A}I
injective,
and
0\longrightarrow L_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\longrightarrow}}V\longrightarrow 0
an exact sequence.
Then there is an exact sequence of the form
:

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\xlongrightarrow{({\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma\\
(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\\
{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(M,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{f}\\
\sigma^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)})}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,Y)\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}C\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

Rewrite the sequence as

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma\\
(M,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\\
{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g}\\
\sigma^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,Y)\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)},{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}C\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}\rightarrow 0.

Since
\phi=0=\psi,

(M,\widetilde{g})\widetilde{f}=0=(N,\widetilde{f})\widetilde{g},
and hence

(M,(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g})\widetilde{f}=0=(N,(M,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{f})\widetilde{g}.
Thus
the following diagrams commute:

\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma\\
(M,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{I\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(M,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(M,(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g})\\
(M,\sigma^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I))\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(M,Y)}

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 32.71532pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.64584pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.55557pt\raise-18.22636pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{g}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-28.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.26768pt\raise 12.61111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g}\\

0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 173.71243pt\raise-28.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-32.71532pt\raise-36.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.8986pt\raise-23.95274pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma)\\
(N,(M,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{f})\end{smallmatrix}\right)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.71532pt\raise-36.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 56.71532pt\raise-36.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 86.71532pt\raise-36.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,Y))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Therefore the map
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma\\
(M,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\\
{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g}\\
\sigma^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is a
\Lambda
-map. Clearly, it is a monomorphism.

Write
\operatorname{Coker}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma\\
(M,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\\
{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g}\\
\sigma^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

as

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}C\\
C^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}
.
Then
C^{\prime}
is the cokernel of
B
-monomorphism
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g}\\
\sigma^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
Since
N_{B}
is flat and
{}_{A}I
is injective, it follows that
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)
is an injective left
B
-module. Thus there is a
B
-map
h
such that the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{g}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,L_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(N,\sigma)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)}

commutes. Therefore

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma)\widetilde{g}\\
\sigma^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1_{(N,I)}&-h\\
0&\pi\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is an exact sequence. It follows that
C^{\prime}\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus V.
This completes the proof. ∎

5.4.
Proof of Theorem
5.2

(1) Since
N_{B}
is flat, by Theorem
3.1
(1),
(^{\perp}\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{Y}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{Y}})
is a cotorsion pair;
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is hereditary.

Since
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is complete, for any
\Lambda
-module
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
,
there is an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow V\longrightarrow L_{2}\longrightarrow 0

with
V\in\mathcal{V}
and
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
. Since
{}_{B}M
is projective, by Lemma
5.6
,
there is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules of the form
:

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}K\\
(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}V\\
V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,\ 1}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\longrightarrow 0

where
{}_{A}P
is projective. Since by assumption
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
,

(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus Y\in\mathcal{Y}
, and hence
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}K\\
(M\otimes_{A}P)\oplus Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}\in\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{Y}}.

On the other hand,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}={\rm T}_{A}P
is a projective
\Lambda
-module, so it is in
\ {}^{\perp}\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\ {}_{B}\mathcal{Y}}.

Also,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}V\\
V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}={\rm T}_{B}V\in{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})
.
Since
N_{B}
is flat and
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{B}(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})=0
, by Lemma
3.8
(2),
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}V\\
V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}={\rm T}_{B}V\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{Y}}.
This shows the completeness of
(^{\perp}\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{Y}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{Y}})
.

Finally, if
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then by Corollary
4.5
(1) one has

{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\mathcal{V})={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})
.

(2) Since
{}_{B}M
is projective, by Theorem
3.1
(2),
(\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp})
is a cotorsion pair; and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is hereditary.

Since
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
is complete, for any
\Lambda
-module
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}
,
there is an exact sequence
0\longrightarrow L_{2}\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow V\longrightarrow 0

with
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
and
V\in\mathcal{V}
. Since
N_{B}
is flat, by Lemma
5.7
, there is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules of the form
:

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,Y)\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}C\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}\rightarrow 0

where
{}_{A}I
is injective. Since
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}
is an injective
\Lambda
-module, it is in
\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}
.
Since
{}_{B}M
is projective and
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{B}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})=0
, it follows from
Lemma
3.8
(4) that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,Y)\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}={\rm H}_{B}Y\in\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}.

Since by assumption
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
,
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\in\mathcal{V}
, and hence
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}C\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\oplus V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}\in\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}.
This proves the completeness of
(\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp})
.

Finally, if
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then by Corollary
4.5
(3) one has
\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp}=\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ \mathcal{Y})={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})
.
\square

5.5.
Lemmas for Theorem
5.4

To see Theorem
5.4
(1), we need

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
.
Suppose
{}_{A}N
is projective. For a
\Lambda
-module
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
,
let
\pi:Q\longrightarrow L_{2}
be an epimorphism with
{}_{B}Q
projective, and

0\longrightarrow X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\longrightarrow}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}L_{1}\longrightarrow 0
an exact sequence.
Then there is an exact sequence of the form
:

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\oplus(N\otimes Q)\\
K\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\\
M\otimes U\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes Q\\
Q\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\xlongrightarrow{(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi^{\prime}\\
f(1\otimes\pi^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}g(1\otimes\pi)\\
\pi\end{smallmatrix}\right))}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Lemma
5.6
. We include the points.
Rewrite the sequence as

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)\\
K\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)\\
(M\otimes_{A}U)\oplus Q\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\xlongrightarrow{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(\pi^{\prime},g(1_{N}\otimes\pi))\\
(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi^{\prime}),\pi)\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\rightarrow 0.

The map
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(\pi^{\prime},g(1_{N}\otimes\pi))\\
(f(1_{M}\otimes\pi^{\prime}),\pi)\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is a
\Lambda
-epimorphism, since the diagrams commute:

\textstyle{(M\otimes_{A}U)\oplus(M\otimes_{A}N\otimes_{B}Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{(1\otimes\pi^{\prime},1_{M}\otimes g(1_{N}\otimes\pi))}
\textstyle{M\otimes_{A}L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{(M\otimes_{A}U)\oplus Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f(1\otimes\pi^{\prime}),\pi)}
\textstyle{L_{2}}

\textstyle{(N\otimes_{B}M\otimes_{A}U)\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{(1_{N}\otimes f(1\otimes\pi^{\prime}),1_{N}\otimes\pi)}
\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{U\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\pi^{\prime},\ g(1_{N}\otimes\pi))}
\textstyle{L_{1}.}

It remains to prove
\operatorname{Ker}(\pi^{\prime},\ g(1_{N}\otimes\pi))\cong X\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)
.
Since
{}_{A}N
is projective,
N\otimes_{B}Q
is a projective left
A
-module. Thus, there is an
A
-map
h
such that the diagram

\textstyle{N\otimes_{B}Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{1_{N}\otimes\pi}
\textstyle{N\otimes L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

commutes. Then

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma&-h\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{U\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\pi^{\prime},\ g(1_{N}\otimes\pi))}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is exact. This completes the proof.∎

To prove Theorem
5.4
(2), we need the following lemma, in which
the second expression of a
\Lambda
-module is more convenient.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
. Suppose
M_{A}
is flat.
For
\Lambda
-module
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}
, let
\sigma:L_{2}\longrightarrow J
be a monomorphism with
{}_{B}J
injective, and

0\longrightarrow L_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\longrightarrow}}U\longrightarrow 0
an exact sequence.
Then there is an exact sequence of the form
:

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\xlongrightarrow{({\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma^{\prime}\\
(N,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{g}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\\
{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(M,\sigma)\widetilde{f}\\
\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right)})}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,X)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
J\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
C\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Lemma
5.7
. We include the points.
First, as in the proof of Lemma
5.7
, one can show that
the map

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma^{\prime}\\
(M,\sigma)\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\\
{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{g}\\
\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\end{smallmatrix}\right):\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,X)\oplus J\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)},{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}

is a
\Lambda
-monomorphism. We omit the details.

Write
\operatorname{Coker}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma^{\prime}\\
(M,\sigma)\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\\
{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(N,\sigma^{\prime})\widetilde{g}\\
\sigma\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

as

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}C^{\prime}\\
C\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}
.
Then
C^{\prime}\cong\operatorname{Coker}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma^{\prime}\\
(M,\sigma)\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
Since
M_{A}
is flat and
{}_{B}J
is injective, it follows that
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)
is an injective left
A
-module. Thus there is an
A
-map
h
such that the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,L_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(M,\sigma^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)}

commutes. Therefore

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\sigma^{\prime}\\
(M,\sigma)\widetilde{f}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{X\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi&0\\
-h&1_{(M,J)}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{U\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is exact, and hence
C^{\prime}\cong U\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J).
∎

5.6.
Proof of Theorem
5.4

The proof is similar to Theorem
5.2
.

(1) Since
M_{A}
is flat, by Theorem
3.1
(1),
({}^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}})
is a cotorsion pair;
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is hereditary.

For any
\Lambda
-module
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
, since
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is complete,
there is an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow U\longrightarrow L_{1}\longrightarrow 0

with
U\in\mathcal{U}
,
X\in\mathcal{X}
.
Since
{}_{A}N
is projective, by Lemma
5.8
, there is an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules of the form
:

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\oplus(N\otimes Q)\\
K\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\\
M\otimes U\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes Q\\
Q\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}\longrightarrow 0

where
{}_{B}Q
is projective. Since by assumption
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, it follows that
X\oplus(N\otimes_{B}Q)\in\mathcal{X}
, and hence
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\oplus(N\otimes Q)\\
K\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\alpha,\beta}\in\binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}.

Since
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes Q\\
Q\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}
, it is in
{}^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}.

Since
M_{A}
is flat and
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})=0
, by Lemma
3.8
(1),
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\\
M\otimes U\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}={\rm T}_{A}U\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}.
Thus,
({}^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \binom{\mathcal{X}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}})
is complete.

Finally if
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then by Corollary
4.5
(2) one has

{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\mathcal{V})={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})
.

(2) Since
{}_{A}N
is projective, by Theorem
3.1
(2),
(\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}^{\perp})
is a cotorsion pair;
and it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is hereditary.

For any
\Lambda
-module
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}
, since
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
is complete,
there is an exact sequence
0\longrightarrow L_{1}\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow U\longrightarrow 0

with
X\in\mathcal{X}
,
U\in\mathcal{U}
. Since
M_{A}
is flat,
by Lemma
5.9
, there is an exact sequence
:

0\rightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,X)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
J\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
C\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}\rightarrow 0

where
{}_{B}J
is injective. Since
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
J\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}
is an injective
\Lambda
-module, it is in

\binom{\ {}_{A}\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}^{\perp}.
Since
{}_{A}N
is projective and
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{A}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})=0
, by Lemma
3.8
(3),
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,X)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}={\rm H}_{A}X\in\ \binom{\ {}_{A}\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}^{\perp}.

Since by assumption
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
,
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\in\mathcal{U}
, and hence
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
C\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}}\in\binom{\ {}_{A}\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}.
So
(\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}^{\perp})
is complete.

Finally if
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
, then by Corollary
4.5
(4) one has

\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{U}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}^{\perp}=\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}).

\square

5.7.
Remark

Under the framework of one of
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and

(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
being an arbitrary complete cotorsion pair, and another being the projective or the injective one, the careful reader will find that the completeness of the following cotorsion pairs

\displaystyle({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)),\ \ \ \ \ \ ({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\ \ \ \ \ \ (\mbox{if}\ M_{A}\ \mbox{and}\ N_{B}\ \mbox{are flat})

\displaystyle(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}),\ \ \ \ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\ \ \ \ \ \ (\mbox{if}\ _{B}M\ \mbox{and}\ _{A}N\ \mbox{are projective})

have not been discussed (also they will be not used in constructing Hovey triples in Section 7).
An interesting special cases of
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
and

({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

have been treated in Theorem
4.6
.

5.8.
Triangular matrix rings

For the case of
M=0
one has

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
0&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be an upper triangular matrix ring.
Suppose that
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
are complete cotorsion pairs
in
A
-
Mod
and
B
-
Mod
, respectively.

(1)
Assume that
\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{B}(N,\ \mathcal{V})=0
. If

N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, then the cotorsion pair

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))=({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is complete.

(2)
Assume that
\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{1}(N,\ \mathcal{X})=0
. If
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ \mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
, then the cotorsion pair

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}))=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))

is complete.

For lower triangle matrix rings (i.e.,
N=0
) one has the similar results. We omit the details. For proof of Proposition
5.10
we need

Lemma 5.11
.

([AA, 3.1])

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an abelian category with enough projectives and injectives.
Assume that
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
be a hereditary cotorsion pair in
\mathcal{C}
,
and
0\longrightarrow X\xlongrightarrow{f}Y\xlongrightarrow{g}Z\longrightarrow 0

be an exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
.

(1)
Assume that
X
and
Z
have special right
\mathcal{A}
-approximation, i.e., there are exact sequences:

0\longrightarrow B_{1}\longrightarrow A_{1}\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow 0,\qquad 0\longrightarrow B_{2}\longrightarrow A_{2}\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow 0,

with
A_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
,
B_{i}\in\mathcal{B}
,
i=1,2
. Then
Y
has a special right
\mathcal{A}
-approximation.

(2)
Assume that
X
and
Z
have special left
\mathcal{B}
-approximation, i.e., there are exact sequences

0\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow B_{1}\longrightarrow A_{1}\longrightarrow 0,\qquad 0\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow B_{2}\longrightarrow A_{2}\longrightarrow 0

with
B_{i}\in\mathcal{B}
,
A_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
,
i=1,2
.
Then
Y
has a special left
\mathcal{B}
-approximation.

Proof of Proposition
5.10
.

(1) By Theorem
4.4
(1),
(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))=({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is a cotorsion pair. Let
\binom{L_{1}}{L_{2}}_{g}
be a
\Lambda
-module. By the completeness of
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
, one has the exact sequences

0\longrightarrow X\xlongrightarrow{\sigma_{1}}U\xlongrightarrow{\pi_{1}}L_{1}\longrightarrow 0,\qquad 0\longrightarrow Y\xlongrightarrow{\sigma_{2}}V\xlongrightarrow{\pi_{2}}L_{2}\longrightarrow 0

in
A
-Mod and
B
-Mod respectively, with
U\in\mathcal{U}
,
X\in\mathcal{X}
,
V\in\mathcal{V}
, and
Y\in\mathcal{Y}
.
Then

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0}\xlongrightarrow{\binom{\sigma_{1}}{0}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0}\xlongrightarrow{\binom{\pi_{1}}{0}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0}\longrightarrow 0

is the special right
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
-approximation of
\binom{L_{1}}{0}_{0}
. Also, since

N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
,

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}V\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1\otimes\sigma_{2}}\xlongrightarrow{\binom{1}{\sigma_{2}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}V\\
V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1}\xlongrightarrow{\binom{0}{\pi_{2}}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0}\longrightarrow 0

is the special right
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
-approximation of
\binom{0}{L_{2}}_{0}
.
Since

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{g}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\longrightarrow 0

is exact, it follows from Lemma
5.11
(1) that

\Lambda
-module
\binom{L_{1}}{L_{2}}_{g}
has a special right
\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
-approximation

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
Y^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{s}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U^{\prime}\\
V^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{h}\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}L_{1}\\
L_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{g}\longrightarrow 0

with
\binom{U^{\prime}}{V^{\prime}}_{h}\in\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})
and
\binom{X^{\prime}}{Y^{\prime}}_{s}\in\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}}
.
This proves the completeness.

The assertion (2) can be similarly proved.

\square

Theorems
5.2
,
5.4
, and Proposition
5.10
are new, even when
M=0
or
N=0
.

6.
Realizations

In Table 1, taking
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
to be the projective cotorsion pair or the injective cotorsion pair, we get Table 2 below.
This section is to show that these cotorsion pairs in Table 2 are pairwise generally different and “new” in some sense. For details see Definitions
4.1
and
6.2
, Propositions
6.1
,
6.3
,
6.9
and
6.10
.
All these results are new, even for
M=0
or
N=0
. Thus, it turns out that Morita rings are rich in producing “new” cotorsion pairs.

6.1.
Cotorsion pairs in Series I in Table 2 are pairwise generally different

To save the space, in Table 2 we use

\mathcal{A}:=A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ \ \mathcal{B}:=B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ \ \mbox{proj.}:=\mbox{projective},

\mathcal{M}:={\rm Mon}(\Lambda)=\Delta(A\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ B\mbox{\rm-Mod})
and
\mathcal{E}:={\rm Epi}(\Lambda)=\nabla(A\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ B\mbox{\rm-Mod}).

About Table 2:
(i) It is clear that (see also Subsection 3.1)

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot})=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod});\ \ \ (^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))=(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}).

(ii) The cotorsion pairs in columns 2 and 3 in Table 2 are
cotorsion pairs in Series I,
and the ones in columns 4 and 5 are the cotorsion pairs in Series II. See Notation
3.3
.

Table 2: Cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda
-Mod

Hereditary cotorsion pairs in Series I

\varphi=0=\psi

Cotorsion pairs in Series II

M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M

(_{B}\mathcal{V},\ _{B}\mathcal{Y})

:

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(N,\mathcal{X})=0

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(M,\mathcal{Y})=0
:

(\binom{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{V}},\binom{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\perp})

(\Delta(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V}),\ \Delta(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V})^{\bot})

(^{\bot}\nabla(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}))

)

,

Even if
{}_{A}N
,
{}_{B}M
proj.,

(^{\bot}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E})\neq(\binom{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}},\ \binom{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\perp})

in general.

(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{A}
)

(\binom{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{B}},\ \binom{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp})

(\Delta(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{B}),(\Delta(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{B})^{\bot}).

If

flat then it is

(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{I}},\ \binom{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{I}})

thus it is

(\Delta(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{B}),\ \binom{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{I}})

If

(\binom{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{B}},\ \binom{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{B}}^{\perp})

thus it is

(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{I}
)

M_{A}
flat:

(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{B}},\ \binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{B}})

proj.:

(\binom{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{P}},\ \binom{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\perp})

(\Delta(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{P}),\Delta(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{P})^{\bot}).

If

flat then it is

(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{B}},\binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{B}})

thus it is

((\Delta(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{P}),\ \binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{B}})

(^{\bot}\nabla(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{B}),\nabla(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{B}))
.

If
{}_{B}M
proj. then it is

(\binom{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{P}},\ \binom{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\perp})

thus it is

(\binom{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{P}},\ \nabla(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{B}))

(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{I})

(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{I})

M_{A}
,
N_{B}
flat:

(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{I}},\ \binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{I}})

(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I})

({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})
.

Even if
M_{A}
,
N_{B}
flat,

(\mathcal{M},\ \mathcal{M}^{\bot})\neq(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{I}},\ \binom{\mathcal{I}}{\mathcal{I}})

in general

(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I})

From the proof of Proposition
6.1
we will see that, in the most cases,
the eight hereditary cotorsion pairs in Series I in Table 2 are pairwise different.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\psi
.
Then the eight hereditary cotorsion pairs in
Series I
in
Table 2
are pairwise generally different, in the sense of
Definition
4.1
.

Proof.

All together there are
\binom{8}{2}=28
situations.

Step 1.
If
A
and
B
are not semisimple,
then the cotorsion pairs in Series I in the same columns are pairwise different. This occupies
2\binom{4}{2}=12
situations.

For example, since
A
is not semisimple,
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\neq\ _{A}\mathcal{I}.

Thus
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),

and hence

(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)).

Step 2.
The projective cotorsion pair
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod})

is generally different from all other seven cotorsion pairs in Series I. This occupies
4
situations.

In fact, taking
{}_{A}N=A=B=\ _{B}M\neq 0
, then

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}

but
\binom{N}{0}_{0,0}\notin\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}
.
Thus
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod})
is generally different from

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}),\ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}),\ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}),\ \ \ (\Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}).

Step 3.
Similarly, the injective cotorsion pair
(\Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I})

is generally different from all other seven cotorsion pairs in Series I. This occupies
3
situations.

Step 4.
For convenience, denote by
R_{\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}}
the cotorsion pair where
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is at the right hand side, i.e.,

R_{\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}}=({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
.
Similarly,
L_{\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
.

Assume that
A
and
B
are not semisimple. Under some extra conditions we will show the following remaining
9
cases (listed in the order of comparing each cotorsion pair with the ones after):

\displaystyle L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}\neq R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}};\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}\neq R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}};\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}\neq R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}};

\displaystyle R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}\neq L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}};\ \ \ \ R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}\neq L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}};\ \ \ \ \ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}\neq R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}};

\displaystyle L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}\neq R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}};\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}\neq L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}};\ \ \ \ \ \ L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}\neq R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}.

To see the inequalities involving
L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\perp})
, it suffices to show

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Since
B
is not semisimple, there is a non-projective
B
-module
Y
. By Lemma
3.8
(2),

{\rm T}_{B}Y=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\in\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),
but

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
This shows
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

Since
A
is not semisimple, there is a non-projective
A
-module
X
. By Lemma
3.8
(1),

{\rm T}_{A}X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
M\otimes_{A}X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\in\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap\ \ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. But
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
M\otimes_{A}X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
This shows
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and

\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

For the next inequalities involving
R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}),

we need to find conditions such that

{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ \ \ \ \ \ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Taking
A=B=M=N\neq 0
and a non-projective
B
-module
Y
,
then
{\rm T}_{B}Y=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\in\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
by Lemma
3.8
(2),
but
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

To see
L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}\neq R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
and
L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}\neq R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}
,
it suffices to show

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

For a non-projective
A
-module
X
,
{\rm T}_{A}X=\binom{X}{M\otimes_{A}X}_{1,0}\in\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap\ ^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}
,
but
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
M\otimes_{A}X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\notin\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
.

Finally, we show that
L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}
is generally different from
R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
and
R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{A}\mathcal{I}}
.
Taking
A=B=M=N\neq 0
and a non-projective
A
-module
X
, it suffices to see

{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

In fact, by Lemma
3.8
(2),
{\rm T}_{A}X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
M\otimes_{A}X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\in\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
;
but
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

This completes the proof. ∎

6.2.
“New” cotorsion pairs in Series I in Table 2

Keep the notations
R_{\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}}
and
L_{\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}}
as in the
Step 4
of the proof of Proposition
6.1
.
Taking off the projective cotorsion pair and
the injective one from Series I of Table 2,
the remaining six hereditary cotorsion pairs

\displaystyle R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)),\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}),

\displaystyle R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)),\ \ \ \ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}),

\displaystyle R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)),\ \ \ \ \ L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})

are “new”, in the following sense.

Definition 6.2
.

A cotorsion pair in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
is said to be “new”, provided that it is generally different from all of the following cotorsion pairs:

\bullet
the projective cotorsion pair
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod});

\bullet
the injective cotorsion pair
(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I});

\bullet
the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair
({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty})
, if
\Lambda
is a Gorenstein ring;

\bullet
the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))
, if
\Lambda
is a Gorenstein ring;

\bullet
the flat cotorsion pair
(_{\Lambda}{\rm F},\ _{\Lambda}{\rm C})
, where
{}_{\Lambda}{\rm F}
is the class of flat
\Lambda
-modules, and

{}_{\Lambda}{\rm C}
is the class of cotorsion
\Lambda
-modules. See [FJ, Lemma 7.1.4].

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
\phi=0=\phi
. Then the following six cotorsion pairs

R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}},\ \ \ R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \ \ R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \ \ L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}

are “new”, in the sense of
Definition
6.2
.

To prove Proposition
6.3
we need some preparations.

Lemma 6.4
.

([GaP, 4.15])
Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
N&A\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
N\otimes_{A}N=0
.
Assume that
{}_{A}N
and
N_{A}
are projective. If
A
is a Gorenstein ring, then so is
\Lambda
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring which is an Artin algebra and a Gorenstein ring, with
\phi=0=\phi
. Then the cotorsion pairs

R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}
and
L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}
are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair and
the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair.

Proof.

Take
\Lambda
to be the Morita ring
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
N&A\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, constructed in Example
4.3
. Thus

A
is the path algebra
k(1\longrightarrow 2)
with
{\rm char}\ k\neq 2
,
N=Ae_{2}\otimes_{k}e_{1}A
, and
N\otimes_{A}N=0
. By Lemma
6.4
,
\Lambda
is a Gorenstein algebra.

Claim 1.
