R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is generally different from
({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty})
.

In fact, since
N\otimes_{A}S_{2}=Ae_{2}\otimes_{k}(e_{1}Ae_{2})=0
,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}S_{2}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}={\rm T}_{A}S_{2}
is a projective
\Lambda
-module, thus

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}S_{2}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
, but
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}S_{2}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Thus
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
, and hence

R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}).

Claim 2.

R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is generally different from
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))
.

In fact, by Example
4.3
one knows

L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}\notin\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

The following
\Lambda
-projective resolution of
{}_{\Lambda}L

0\longrightarrow\left(\begin{smallmatrix}S_{2}\\
S_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\binom{\sigma}{-1}\\
\binom{1}{-\sigma}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\oplus S_{2}\\
S_{2}\oplus Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(1,\sigma)\\
(\sigma,1)\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}\longrightarrow 0

shows that
{\rm proj.dim}_{\Lambda}L=1
.
So
L\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
, and hence
{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
. Thus

R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

Claim 3.

L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})

is generally different from
({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty})
.

In fact, by Example
4.3
one knows

L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}.

By
Claim 2
,
L\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
. Thus

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp}\neq\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
, and hence

L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\neq({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}).

Claim 4.

L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})

is generally different from
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))
.

In fact, since
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,S_{1})=0
,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
S_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}={\rm H}_{B}S_{1}
is an injective
\Lambda
-module, thus

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
S_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}^{<\infty}=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
,
but
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
S_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
Thus
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
, and hence

L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\neq(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

This completes the proof. ∎

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring which is an Artin algebra and a Gorenstein ring, with
\phi=0=\phi
. Then the cotorsion pairs

R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \ \ R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \ \ L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}

are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair and
the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair.

Proof.

Choose quasi-Frobenius rings
A
and
B
, bimodules
{}_{B}M_{A}
and
{}_{A}N_{B}
, satisfying the following conditions (i), (ii), (iii), (iv):

(i)
A
and
B
are quasi-Frobenius and not semisimple;

(ii)
{}_{A}N
and
{}_{B}M
are non-zero projective modules, and
M_{A}
and
N_{B}
are flat;

(iii)
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M;

(iv)
\Lambda
is a noetherian ring.

By Remark
4.7
, such
\Lambda
’s exist! By Theorem
4.6
,
\Lambda
is a Gorenstein ring with
{\rm inj.dim}\Lambda\leq 1
, the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair
({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ \mathcal{P}^{\leq 1})
is exactly

({}^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}})
, and the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))
is exactly
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
.

Claim 1.

R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}
and
R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}

are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair.

Since
A
and
B
are not semisimple,
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\neq\ _{A}\mathcal{I}
and
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\neq\ _{B}\mathcal{I}
. Thus
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, and hence

R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1})

and

R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}).

Claim 2.

L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
and
L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}

are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair.

Since
A
is not semisimple, there is a non-projective
A
-module
X
. By Lemma
3.8
(1),

{\rm T}_{A}X=\binom{X}{M\otimes_{A}X}_{1,0}\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}
,
but
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
M\otimes_{A}X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\notin\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
,
which shows
\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}\neq\ ^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}.

Hence

L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\neq({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}).

Similarly,
L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\neq({}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}).

Claim 3.

R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}
and
R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
are generally different from the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair.

Since
B
is not semisimple, there is a non-projective
B
-module
Y
. Then

{\rm T}_{B}Y=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\in\ ^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
by Lemma
3.8
(2), but

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\notin\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
This shows
{}^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
Thus

R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

Similarly,
R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\neq(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

Claim 4.

L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
and
L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}

are generally different from the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair.

Since
B
is not semisimple,
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\neq\ _{B}\mathcal{P}
. Thus
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\neq\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, and hence

L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\neq(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

Similarly,
L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})\neq(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

This completes the proof. ∎

We also need the following result due to P. A. Krylov and E. Yu. Yardykov [KY].

Lemma 6.7
.

([KY, Corollary 2.5])

Let
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{f,g}
be a flat
\Lambda
-module. Then
\operatorname{Coker}g
is a flat
A
-module and
\operatorname{Coker}f
is a flat
B
-module.

Proof of Proposition
6.3
.
By Proposition
6.1
, these six cotorsion pairs
are generally different from the projective cotorsion pair and the injective one; and
they are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair and the Gorenstein-injective one, by Lemmas
6.5
and
6.6
. It remains to show that they are generally different from the flat cotorsion pair.

In fact, choose rings
A
and
B
such that they admit non flat modules (such a ring
A
of course exists! For example, just take a finite-dimensional algebra
A
which is not semisimple. Then

A
has a finitely generated module
M
which is not projective, and
M
is not flat). Taking non-flat modules
{}_{A}X
and
{}_{B}Y
, by Lemma
6.7
, all the following
\Lambda
-modules are not flat:

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0},\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0},\ \ {\rm T}_{A}X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
M\otimes_{A}X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0},\ \ {\rm T}_{B}Y=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}.

However,

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
, one has
{\rm T}_{A}X=\binom{X}{M\otimes_{A}X}_{1,0}\in{}^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}
, by Lemma
3.8
(1).

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}}=(^{\perp}\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}})
,
one has
{\rm T}_{B}Y=\binom{N\otimes_{B}Y}{Y}_{0,1}\in\ ^{\perp}\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{I}}
, by Lemma
3.8
(2).

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}=(\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}^{\perp}),
one has

\binom{0}{Y}_{0,0}\in\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{P}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
.

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}=({}^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}})
, one has
{\rm T}_{A}X=\binom{X}{M\otimes_{A}X}_{1,0}\in{}^{\perp}\binom{{}_{A}\mathcal{I}}{B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}
, by Lemma
3.8
(1).

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}},\ \binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\perp})
, one has

\binom{X}{0}_{0,0}\in\binom{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}}{{}_{B}\mathcal{P}}
.

In conclusion, the five cotorsion pairs
R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \ R_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}},\ \ L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \ R_{{}_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}},\ \ L_{A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}
are generally different from the flat cotorsion pair.

Finally, for the cotorsion pair
L_{{}_{A}\mathcal{P},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}}=(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})
,
we take
\Lambda
to be the Morita ring and
L=\binom{Ae_{1}}{Ae_{1}}_{\sigma,\sigma}
, as given in Example
4.3
. Then
L\in\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{}_{A}\mathcal{P}\\
{}_{B}\mathcal{P}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
But
L
is not a flat
\Lambda
-module (otherwise, since
L
is finitely generated,
L
is projective, which is absurd).

This completes the proof.
\square

6.3.
Cotorsion pairs in Series II in Table 2 are pairwise generally different

Also, in the most cases, the eight cotorsion pairs in Series II in Table 2 are pairwise different.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
,
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X}^{\prime})
cotorsion pairs in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
cotorsion pairs in
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.
Then

(1)

\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V})=\Delta(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{V}^{\prime})
if and only if
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}^{\prime}.

(2)

\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})=\nabla(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
if and only if
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(1)
This follows from the fact

{\rm T}_{A}U=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}U\\
M\otimes_{A}U\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\in\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \ \forall\ U\in\mathcal{U};\ \ \ \ \ {\rm T}_{B}V=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}V\\
V\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\in\Delta(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{V}),\ \ \forall\ V\in\mathcal{V}.

(2)
This follows from the fact
{\rm H}_{A}X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,X)\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}\in\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \ \forall\ X\in\mathcal{X},
and
{\rm H}_{B}Y=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,Y)\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\in\nabla(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y}),\ \ \forall\ Y\in\mathcal{Y},
here we use the second expression of
\Lambda
-modules. ∎

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.
Then the eight cotorsion pairs in
Series II
in
Table 2
are pairwise generally different.

Proof.

All together there are
\binom{8}{2}=28
situations.

Step 1.
By Lemma
6.8
, the cotorsion pairs in Series II in the same columns of Table 2 are pairwise different. This occupies
2\binom{4}{2}=12
situations.

Step 2.

(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod})

is generally different from all other cotorsion pairs in Series II in Table 2. This occupies
4
situations.

In fact, taking
A
to be a non semisimple ring and
N
a non injective
A
-module. Then
\binom{N}{0}_{0,0}\in\Lambda
-Mod.
Since the map
0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,N)
is not epic, it follows that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\notin{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\notin\nabla(A\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\notin\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{\rm-Mod}),\ \ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\notin\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}.

So
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P},\ \Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod})
is generally different from
(^{\perp}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))
,
(^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))
,
(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{\rm-Mod}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{\rm-Mod}))
, and

(\Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I})
.

Step 3.
Similarly,
(\Lambda\mbox{\rm-Mod},\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I})

is generally different from other cotorsion pairs in Series II. This occupies
3
situations.

Step 4.
Assume that
M\neq 0\neq N
. It remains to show the following
9
cases:

\displaystyle{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)\neq\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp};\ \ \ \ \ \ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda)\neq\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp};\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda)\neq{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\perp};

\displaystyle\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}\neq\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I});\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}\neq\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod});

\displaystyle\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\neq\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp};\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\neq{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\perp};

\displaystyle\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp}\neq\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod});\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})\neq{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\perp}.

First, we see the inequalities involving
{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)=\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
.
Let
{}_{A}I
be the injective envelope of
{}_{A}N
. By Lemma
3.10
(1) one has

{\rm Z}_{A}I=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}\cap\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp}\cap\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}.

But
\widetilde{g}:0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)
is not epic, so
\binom{I}{0}_{0,0}\notin\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})={\rm Epi}(\Lambda)
.
This shows
{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)\neq\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}
,
{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)\neq\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp}
and
{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)\neq{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\perp}
.

Next, we see the two inequalities involving
\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}.

By Lemma
3.10
(1),

{\rm Z}_{A}N=\binom{N}{0}_{0,0}\in\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}
. But
\widetilde{g}:0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,N)\neq 0
is not epic, so
\binom{N}{0}_{0,0}
is not in
\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})
and
\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
.
This shows the two inequalities.

Next, to see
\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\neq\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp}
and
\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\neq\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}
,
Let
{}_{B}J
be the injective envelope of
{}_{B}M
. By Lemma
3.10
(2),

{\rm Z}_{B}J=\binom{0}{J}_{0,0}
is in
\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp}
and
\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}
.
But
\widetilde{f}:0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)
is not epic, so
\binom{0}{J}_{0,0}\notin\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})
.

Finally, to see the two inequalities involving
\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
.
Let
{}_{A}I
be the injective envelope of
{}_{A}N
. By Lemma
3.10
(1),

{\rm Z}_{A}I=\binom{I}{0}_{0,0}
is in
\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp}
and
\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}
.
But
\widetilde{g}:0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)
is not epic, so
\binom{I}{0}_{0,0}\notin\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
.
This shows
\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp}\neq\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})

and
\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})\neq{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\perp}.

∎

6.4.
“New” cotorsion pairs in Series II in Table 2

In Series II of Table 2, taking off the projective cotorsion pair and
the injective one, the remaining six cotorsion pairs
are “new”.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.
Then all the six cotorsion pairs

\displaystyle({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot}),\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ (^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))

\displaystyle(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\bot}),\ \ \ \ \ \ (^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))

\displaystyle(\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot}),\ \ \ \ \ \ (^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))

are “new”, in the sense of
Definition
6.2
.

To prove Proposition
6.10
, we first show

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring which is an Artin algebra and a Gorenstein ring, with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.
Then the cotorsion pairs

({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})
and
(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))
are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair
and the Gorenstein-injective one.

Proof.

Take
\Lambda
to be the Morita ring
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
N&A\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, constructed in Example
4.3
. Thus

A
is the path algebra
k(1\longrightarrow 2)
with
{\rm char}\ k\neq 2
,
N=Ae_{2}\otimes_{k}e_{1}A
, and
N\otimes_{A}N=0
. By Lemma
6.4
,
\Lambda
is a Gorenstein algebra.

Claim 1.

({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})

is generally different from
({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty})
.

In fact,
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}\in{\rm Mon}(\Lambda).

By
Claim 2
in the proof of Lemma
6.5
,
{\rm proj.dim}_{\Lambda}L=1.
Thus
L
is not Gorenstein-projective (otherwise
L
is projective, which is absurd. Note that a Gorenstein-projective module of finite projective dimension is projective. See [EJ, 10.2.3]).
So
L\notin{\rm GP}(\Lambda)
. Thus
{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)\neq{\rm GP}(\Lambda)
, and hence

({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})\neq({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}).

Claim 2.

({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})

is generally different from
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))
.

In fact, the following
\Lambda
-projective resolution of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}

0\longrightarrow{\rm T}_{B}S_{2}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
S_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\binom{0}{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm T}_{A}(Ae_{1})=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
S_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\longrightarrow 0

shows that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
.
Since
N\otimes_{A}Ae_{1}\cong S_{2}
,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\notin{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)
.
Thus
{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)\neq\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
, and hence

({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})\neq(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

Claim 3.

(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))

is generally different from
({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty})
.

In fact, by
Claim 2
,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
. Since
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,Ae_{1})\cong S_{1}\neq 0
(cf. Example
4.3
),

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0}\notin{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)
.
Thus

{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)\neq\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
, and hence

(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))\neq({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}).

Claim 4.

(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))

is generally different from
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))
.

In fact,
L=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}Ae_{1}\\
Ae_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\sigma,\sigma}\in{\rm Epic}(\Lambda)
and
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(L,L)\neq 0
(cf. Example
4.3
).
So
L\notin\ ^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)
. However,
L\in\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
.
Thus
{}^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)\neq\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty}
, and hence

(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))\neq(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

This completes the proof. ∎

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring which is an Artin algebra and a Gorenstein ring, with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.
Then the cotorsion pairs

\displaystyle(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\bot}),\ \ \ \ (^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))

\displaystyle(\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot}),\ \ \ \ (^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))

are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair
and the Gorenstein-injective one.

Proof.

Choose rings
A
and
B
, bimodules
{}_{B}M_{A}
and
{}_{A}N_{B}
, such that

(i)
A
and
B
are quasi-Frobenius and not semisimple;

(ii)
{}_{A}N
and
{}_{B}M
are non-zero projective modules, and
M_{A}
and
N_{B}
are flat;

(iii)
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M;

(iv)
\Lambda
is an Artin algebra.

By Remark
4.7
, such
\Lambda
’s always exist! By Theorem
4.6
,
\Lambda
is a Gorenstein ring with
{\rm inj.dim}\Lambda\leq 1
,
({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ \mathcal{P}^{\leq 1})=({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})
,
and
(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))=(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))
.

Claim 1.

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\bot})
and
(\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot})
are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair.

In fact, since
A
and
B
are not semisimple,
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\neq\ _{A}\mathcal{P}
and

B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\neq\ _{B}\mathcal{P}
. By Lemma
6.8
,

\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})\neq\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})={\rm Mon}(\Lambda)
, and

\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})\neq{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)
.
Thus

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\bot})\neq({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ \mathcal{P}^{\leq 1})

and

(\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot})\neq({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ \mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}).

Claim 2.

(^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))

and
(^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))
are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair.

In fact, by Lemma
3.10
(3),

{\rm Z}_{A}A=\binom{A}{0}_{0,0}\in\ ^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\cap\ ^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
.
But
f:M\otimes_{A}A\longrightarrow 0
is not monic, so
\binom{A}{0}_{0,0}\notin\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})={\rm Mon}(\Lambda)
.
This shows
{}^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\neq{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)
and
{}^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})\neq{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)
.
Thus

(^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))\neq({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ \mathcal{P}^{\leq 1})

and

(^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))\neq({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})=({\rm GP}(\Lambda),\ \mathcal{P}^{\leq 1}).

Claim 3.

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\bot})
and
(\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot})
are generally different from the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair.

In fact, let
I
be the injective envelope of
{}_{A}N
. By Lemma
3.10
(1),

{\rm Z}_{A}I=\binom{I}{0}_{0,0}\in\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}\cap\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\perp}
.
But
\widetilde{g}:0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)
is not epic, so
\binom{I}{0}_{0,0}\notin\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})={\rm Epi}(\Lambda)
.
This shows
\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}\neq{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)
and
\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp}\neq{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)
.
Thus

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\bot})\neq(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))

and

(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\bot})\neq(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

Claim 4.

(^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))
and
(^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))
are generally different from the Gorenstein-injective cotorsion pair.

In fact, since
A
and
B
are not semisimple,
{}_{A}\mathcal{I}\neq A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
and

{}_{B}\mathcal{I}\neq B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. By Lemma
6.8
,

\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})\neq\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})={\rm Epi}(\Lambda)

and
\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\neq{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)
.
Thus

(^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))\neq(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda))

and

(^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))\neq(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))=(_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}^{\leq 1},\ {\rm GI}(\Lambda)).

This completes the proof. ∎

Proof of Proposition
6.10
.
By Proposition
6.9
, these six cotorsion pairs
are generally different from the projective cotorsion pair and the injective one.
By Lemmas
6.11
and
6.12
, they are generally different from the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair
and the Gorenstein-injective one. It remains to show that they are generally different from the flat cotorsion pair.

In fact, choose rings
A
and
B
such that they admit non flat modules (such a ring of course exists! See the proof of Proposition
6.3
). Taking non flat modules
{}_{A}X
and
{}_{B}Y
, by Lemma
6.7
, all the following
\Lambda
-modules are not flat:

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0},\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,0},\ \ {\rm T}_{A}X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}X\\
M\otimes_{A}X\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{1,0},\ \ {\rm T}_{B}Y=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Y\\
Y\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{0,1}.

However,

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
({\rm Mon}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Mon}(\Lambda)^{\bot})
, one has
{\rm T}_{A}X=\binom{X}{M\otimes_{A}X}_{1,0}\in{\rm Mon}(\Lambda)
.

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
(\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})^{\perp})
,
one has
{\rm T}_{B}Y=\binom{N\otimes_{B}Y}{Y}_{0,1}\in\Delta(_{A}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
.

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
(^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I}))
, one has

\binom{0}{Y}_{0,0}\in\ ^{\perp}\nabla(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})
, by Lemma
3.10
(4).

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
(\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})^{\bot})
, one has
{\rm T}_{A}X=\binom{X}{M\otimes_{A}X}_{1,0}\in\Delta(A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{P})
.

\bullet
For the cotorsion pair
(^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}),\ \nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}))
, one has

\binom{X}{0}_{0,0}\in\ ^{\bot}\nabla(_{A}\mathcal{I},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
, by Lemma
3.10
(3).

In conclusion, the five cotorsion pairs are different from the flat cotorsion pair.

Finally, to see
(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))
is generally different from the flat cotorsion pair, choose a ring
A
such that
A
admits a flat (left) module which is not projective.

(For example, the ring
\mathbb{Z}
of integers has a flat module
{}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, but
{}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is not projective, or equivalently,
{}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is not free.)

Let

\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&0\\
0&A\end{smallmatrix}\right)=A\times A
. Then
{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)=\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, and hence

{}^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}
. By the choice of
A
,
{}^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda)=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}
is strictly contained in
{}_{\Lambda}{\rm F}
, the class of flat
\Lambda
-modules. It follows that

(^{\bot}{\rm Epi}(\Lambda),\ {\rm Epi}(\Lambda))
is generally different from the flat cotorsion pair.
\square

7.
Abelian model structures on Morita rings

Based on results in the previous sections, we will see how abelian model structures on
A
-Mod and
B
-Mod induce abelian model structures on Morita rings; and
we will see that all these abelian model structures obtained on Morita rings are pairwise generally different, and they are generally different from the six well-known abelian model structures (cf. Proposition
7.20
).

7.1.
Cofibrantly generated Hovey triples in Morita rings

Let
R
be a ring. Recall that a Hovey triple
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W})
in
R\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
is cofibrantly generated,
if both the cotorsion pairs
(\mathcal{C}\cap\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})
and
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{W})
are cogenerated by sets.
If a model structure on
R\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
is clear in context, we write Quillen’s homotopy category simply as
{\rm Ho}(R)
.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
,
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{W}_{1})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{W}_{2})
cofibrantly generated
Hovey triples in
A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
and
B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, respectively.

(1)
Suppose that
{\rm Tor}^{A}_{1}(M,\ \mathcal{U}^{\prime})=0={\rm Tor}^{B}_{1}(N,\ \mathcal{V}^{\prime})
,
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1}.
Then

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a cofibrantly generated Hovey triple in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod};
and it is hereditary with
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(A)\oplus{\rm Ho}(B),
provided that
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{W}_{1})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{W}_{2})
are hereditary.

(2)
Suppose that
{\rm Ext}_{B}^{1}(M,\mathcal{Y})=0={\rm Ext}_{A}^{1}(N,\mathcal{X})
,
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}
. Then

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a cofibrantly generated
Hovey triple
;
and it is hereditary with
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(A)\oplus{\rm Ho}(B)
, provided that
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{W}_{1})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{W}_{2})
are hereditary.

Proof.
Put
\mathcal{U}:=\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1},\ \ \mathcal{X}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1},\ \ \mathcal{V}:=\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2},\ \ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}.

Since
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{W}_{1})
is a cofibrantly generated Hovey triple in
A
-Mod,

(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X}^{\prime})
are cotorsion pairs in
A
-Mod, cogenerated by, say,
set
S_{1}
and set
S_{1}^{\prime}
, respectively. Similarly,
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
are cotorsion pairs in
B
-Mod, cogenerated by, say, set
S_{2}
and set
S_{2}^{\prime}
, respectively.

(1) Since
{\rm Tor}^{A}_{1}(M,\ \mathcal{U})\subseteq{\rm Tor}^{A}_{1}(M,\ \mathcal{U}^{\prime})=0

and
{\rm Tor}^{B}_{1}(N,\ \mathcal{V})\subseteq{\rm Tor}^{B}_{1}(N,\ \mathcal{V}^{\prime})=0
,
it follows from Theorem
3.1
(1) that
(^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}},\ \binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}})
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda
-Mod; and it is cogenerated by set
{\rm T}_{A}(S_{1})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(S_{2})
, by Proposition
5.1
(1).

Since
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}\subseteq M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
and
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}\subseteq N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X},
by Theorem
4.4
(1),
{}^{\perp}\binom{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{Y}}={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V})
.
Thus,
({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is
a cotorsion pair, cogenerated by set
{\rm T}_{A}(S_{1})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(S_{2})
.

Similarly,
({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is a cotorsion pair,
cogenerated by set
{\rm T}_{A}(S^{\prime}_{1})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(S^{\prime}_{2})
.

Since
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}
, one has

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2})={\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}).

Also,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Since
\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
are thick,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

is thick.
Thus

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a cofibrantly generated Hovey triple.

If
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{W}_{1})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{W}_{2})
are hereditary Hovey triples, then so is
({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
. Since
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1},

by Theorem
2.8
one has

\displaystyle{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\cong(({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))/(({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

\displaystyle\cong({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}))/(({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y}))

\displaystyle\cong[{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})/{\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{X})]\oplus[{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y})]

\displaystyle\cong[(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})/(\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{X})]\oplus[(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/(\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y})]

\displaystyle={\rm Ho}(A)\oplus{\rm Ho}(B).

(2)
Since
{\rm Ext}_{A}^{1}(M,\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})\subseteq{\rm Ext}_{A}^{1}(M,\ \mathcal{Y})=0

and
{\rm Ext}_{B}^{1}(N,\ \mathcal{X}^{\prime})\subseteq{\rm Ext}_{B}^{1}(N,\ \mathcal{X})=0
, by Theorem
3.1
(2),
(\binom{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}},\ \binom{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}}^{\perp})
is a cotorsion pair in
\Lambda
-Mod.

Since
{\rm Hom}_{B}(M,\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{B}(M,\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
and
{\rm Hom}_{A}(N,\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{A}(N,\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\prime},
by Theorem
4.4
(2) one has

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\perp})=(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))

and
\binom{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}}^{\perp}={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
. By Proposition
5.1
(2),
(^{\perp}\nabla(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime}),\ \nabla(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))

is cogenerated by set
{\rm Z}_{A}(S^{\prime}_{1})\oplus{\rm Z}_{B}(S^{\prime}_{2})
.
Thus,
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))
is
a cotorsion pair, cogenerated by set
{\rm Z}_{A}(S^{\prime}_{1})\oplus{\rm Z}_{B}(S^{\prime}_{2})
.

Similarly,
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))
is
a cotorsion pair, cogenerated by set
{\rm Z}_{A}(S_{1})\oplus{\rm Z}_{B}(S_{2})
.

Note that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Since
{\rm Hom}_{A}(N,\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
and
{\rm Hom}_{B}(M,\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{1}
, one has

({\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{W}_{1})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}_{2})={\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}).

Since
\mathcal{W}_{1}
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
are thick,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

is thick.
Thus

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a cofibrantly generated Hovey triple.

If
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{W}_{1})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{W}_{2})
are hereditary, then
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is hereditary. Since
{\rm Hom}_{A}(N,\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
and
{\rm Hom}_{B}(M,\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime},

by Theorem
2.8
one has

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {\rm Ho}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\cong(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap({\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})))/(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap({\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})))

\displaystyle\cong({\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}))/(({\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y}))

\displaystyle\cong[{\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})/{\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{X})]\oplus[{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y})]

\displaystyle\cong[(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})/(\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{X})]\oplus[(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/(\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y})]

\displaystyle={\rm Ho}(A)\oplus{\rm Ho}(B).\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \square

From Theorem
7.1
and its proof, one easily sees the following.

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.
Let
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
be
compatible hereditary cotorsion pairs in
A
-
Mod
, cogenerated by sets
S_{1}
and
S_{1}^{\prime}
, respectively, with Gillespie-Hovey triple
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{W}_{1})
.
Let
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
be
compatible hereditary cotorsion pairs in
B
-
Mod
, cogenerated by sets
S_{2}
and
S_{2}^{\prime}
, respectively, with Gillespie-Hovey triple
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{W}_{2})
.

(1)
Assume that
{\rm Tor}^{A}_{1}(M,\ \mathcal{U}^{\prime})=0={\rm Tor}^{B}_{1}(N,\ \mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \ M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},\ N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}.
Then

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ ({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

are compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(A)\oplus{\rm Ho}(B).

(2)
Assume that
{\rm Ext}_{B}^{1}(M,\mathcal{Y})=0={\rm Ext}_{A}^{1}(N,\mathcal{X})
,
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\mathcal{Y})\subseteq\mathcal{U}
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
. Then

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))

are compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{1}\\
\mathcal{W}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(A)\oplus{\rm Ho}(B).

7.2.
Hovey triples in Morita rings

We stress that, all the results in the rest of this section are not consequences of Theorem
7.1
, or Corollary
7.2
,
since they need module-theoretical arguments on the completeness of cotorsion pairs in Morita rings, developed in Section 5. Thus, all these results are new even for
M=0
or
N=0
.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.
Let
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{W})
be a Hovey triple in
B
-
Mod
. Suppose that
N_{B}
is flat and
{}_{B}M
is projective.

(1)
If
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}
, then

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a Hovey triple in
\Lambda
-
Mod
;
and it is hereditary with
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(B)
, provided that

(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{W})
is hereditary.

(2)
If
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}
, then

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a Hovey triple in
\Lambda
-
Mod
;
and it is hereditary with
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(B)
, provided that

(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{W})
is hereditary.

Proof.

Put
\mathcal{V}:=\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W},\ \ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}.

Since
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{W})
is a Hovey triple in
B
-
Mod,

(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
are complete cotorsion pairs in
B
-
Mod.

(1)
Since
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y},

it follows from Theorem
5.2
(1) that

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is a complete cotorsion pair in
\Lambda
-Mod.
Similarly,
({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is a complete cotorsion pair.

Since
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{W}
, it follows that

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W})={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}).

Clearly,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
Since
\mathcal{W}
is a thick class of
B
-
Mod,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is a thick class of
\Lambda
-
Mod.
By definition

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a Hovey triple.

If
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{W})
is a hereditary Hovey triple,
then by Theorem
5.2
(1), both
({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
and
({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
are hereditary cotorsion pairs, and hence
({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is a hereditary Hovey triple. By Theorem
2.8
one has

\displaystyle{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\cong(({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))/(({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

\displaystyle\cong({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}))/(({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\cong{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\cong(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})\cong{\rm Ho}(B).

(2)
The proof is similar as
(1)
. We include the main steps. Since
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
,
by Theorem
5.2
(2),
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))
is a complete cotorsion pair.
Similarly,
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))
is a complete cotorsion pair.

Clearly
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Since

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{W}
, it follows that

({\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}\cap\mathcal{W})={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}).

Also,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is a thick class of
\Lambda
-
Mod. By definition

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a Hovey triple. Moreover, it is hereditary if
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{W})
is hereditary. In this case,
by Theorem
2.8
one has

\displaystyle{\rm Ho}(\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})

\displaystyle\cong(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap({\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})))/(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap({\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y})))

\displaystyle\cong({\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}))/({\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\cong{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\cong(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})\cong{\rm Ho}(B).

∎

From Theorem
7.3
and its proof, one has

Corollary 7.4
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
,
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})

compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
B
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{W})
. Suppose that
N_{B}
is flat and
{}_{B}M
is projective.

(1)
If
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
, then

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ ({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

are compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(B)
.

(2)
If
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
, then

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))

are compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(B)
.

Similar as Theorem
7.3
, starting from a Hovey triple in
A
-Mod and using Theorem
5.4
, we get

Theorem 7.5
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
.
Let
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{W})
be a Hovey triple in
A
-
Mod
.
Suppose that
M_{A}
is flat and
{}_{A}N
is projective.

(1)
If
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{W}
, then

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a Hovey triple
;
and it is hereditary with
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(A)
, provided that

(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{W})
is hereditary.

(2)
If
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{W},
then

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a Hovey triple
;
and it is hereditary with
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(A)
, provided that

(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{W})
is hereditary.

Corollary 7.6
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
,
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})

compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
A
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{W})
. Suppose that
M_{A}
is flat and
{}_{A}N
is projective.

(1)
If
N\otimes_{B}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, then

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ ({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

are compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(A)
.

(2)
If
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,\ _{B}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{U},
then

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}))

are compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(A)
.

7.3.
Gillespie-Hovey triples in Morita rings, via generalized projective (injective) cotorsion pairs

The notion of generalized projective (injective) cotorsion pairs is essentially due to H. Becker [Bec].

Definition 7.7
.

(1)
A complete cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
in an abelian category
\mathcal{A}
with enough projective objects
is
a generalized projective cotorsion pair
, or in short, gpctp, provided that

{\rm(i)}

\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{P}
, where
\mathcal{P}
is the class of projective objects of
\mathcal{A};

{\rm(ii)}
the class
\mathcal{Y}
is thick.

{\rm(1^{\prime})}
A complete cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
in an abelian category
\mathcal{A}
with enough injective objects is
a generalized injective cotorsion pair
, or in short, gictp, provided that

{\rm(i^{\prime})}

\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{I}
, where
\mathcal{I}
is the class of injective objects of
\mathcal{A};

{\rm(ii^{\prime})}
the class
\mathcal{X}
is thick.

Example-Remark 7.8
.

A
gpctp

(
respectively,
gictp
)
is not necessarily the projective
(
respectively, injective
)
cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{A})

(
respectively,
(\mathcal{A},\ \mathcal{I}))
.

(1)

([H2])
For a Gorenstein ring
R
, the Gorenstein-projective cotorsion pair

({\rm GP}(R),\ _{R}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty})
is a
gpctp
. Dually,

(_{R}\mathcal{P}^{<\infty},\ {\rm GI}(R))
is a
gictp
.

(2)
Let
{\rm Ch}(R)
be the complex category of modules over ring
R
,
\mathcal{E}
the class of
acyclic complexes, and
{\rm dg}\mathcal{P}
the class of dg projective complexes
Q

(
see
[Sp], [AF]
)
, i.e., components of
Q
are projective and
\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}(Q,\mathcal{E})
is acyclic.
By
[EJX]
,
({\rm dg}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{E})
is a cotorsion pair, and

{\rm dg}\mathcal{P}\cap\mathcal{E}
is exactly the class of projective objects of
{\rm Ch}(R)
. That is,

{\rm dg}\mathcal{P}\cap\mathcal{E}=\{\bigoplus\limits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{i}(P)\ |\ P\in\ _{R}\mathcal{P}\}

where
P^{i}(P):\ \cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Id}}}{{\rightarrow}}P\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots
is the complex with
i
-th and
(i+1)
-th component
P
. By
[Sp]

(
also
[BN]
)
, for any complex
X
there is
an epimorphism
Q\longrightarrow X
which is a quasi-isomorphism. Thus,
({\rm dg}\mathcal{P},\ \mathcal{E})
is complete, and hence generalized projective.
Dually, there is a
gictp

(\mathcal{E},\ {\rm dg}\mathcal{I})
. See
[Gil1]
for an important development of this work.

(3)
Any
gpctp

(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
is hereditary,
\mathcal{X}
is a Frobenius category
(
with the canonical exact structure
)
, and
\mathcal{P}
is
the class of projective-injective objects.

(3^{\prime})
Any
gictp

(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
is hereditary,
\mathcal{Y}
is a Frobenius category, and
\mathcal{I}
is
the class of projective-injective objects.

Taking gpctps or gictps in Corollary
7.4
, we get a stronger and an improved result without extra conditions (i.e., the conditions
“
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
” and “
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
” in Corollary
7.4
can be dropped).
This is the reason we list it as a theorem.

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
. Suppose that
N_{B}
is flat and
{}_{B}M
is projective.

(1)
Let
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
be compatible gpctps in
B
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{W})
.
Then

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ ({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

are compatible gpctps in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/_{B}\mathcal{P}\cong{\rm Ho}(B)
.

(2)
Let
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})
and
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
be compatible gictps in
B
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{W})
. Then

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))

are compatible gictps in
\Lambda
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}),\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/_{B}\mathcal{I}\cong{\rm Ho}(B)
.

Proof.

(1)
Since
{}_{B}M
is projective,
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\ _{B}\mathcal{P}.
Since cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime})
is generalized projective,

M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\ _{B}\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}.

Thus, by Corollary
7.4
(1),

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ ({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

are compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{W}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(B)\cong(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y})/_{B}\mathcal{P}
. Since

{}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P})=\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P\\
M\otimes_{A}P\end{smallmatrix}\right)\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}N\otimes_{B}Q\\
Q\end{smallmatrix}\right)\ |\ P\in\ _{A}\mathcal{P},\ Q\in\ _{B}\mathcal{P}\}

and
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
, it follows that

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}))\cap\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y})={\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P})=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{P}.

Since
\mathcal{Y}
is thick,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is thick.
Thus, cotorsion pair
({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is generalized projective.
Similarly,
({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right))
is generalized projective.

(2)
Since
N_{B}
is flat,
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\ _{B}\mathcal{I}
.
Since
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})
is generalized injective,

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\ _{B}\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
.

Thus, by Corollary
7.4
(2),

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}))

are compatible complete hereditary cotorsion pairs, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong{\rm Ho}(B)\cong(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})/_{A}\mathcal{I}
.
Since

{}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I})=\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,I)\end{smallmatrix}\right)\oplus\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,J)\\
J\end{smallmatrix}\right)\ |\ I\in\ _{A}\mathcal{I},\ J\in\ _{B}\mathcal{I}\}

and
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(N,\ _{A}\mathcal{I})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
,
it follows that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cap({\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{Y})={\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I})=\ _{\Lambda}\mathcal{I}.

Since
\mathcal{V}
is thick,
\binom{A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}}{\mathcal{V}}
is thick.
Thus
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}))
is generalized injective. Similarly,
(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}\\
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{I})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}))
is generalized injective.
∎

Similarly, taking gpctps or gictps in Corollary
7.6
, we get a stronger and an improved result.

Theorem 7.10
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
. Suppose that
M_{A}
is flat and
{}_{A}N
is projective.

(1)
Let
(\mathcal{U},\ \mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X}^{\prime})
be compatible gpctps in
A
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{W})
.
Then

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ ({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

are compatible gpctps in
\Lambda\mbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

({\rm T}_{A}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{P}),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{X}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})/_{A}\mathcal{P}\cong{\rm Ho}(A)
.

(2)
Let
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{X})
and
(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
be compatible gictps in
A
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\ \mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{W})
. Then

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}))\ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ (\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}))

are compatible gictps in
\Lambda
-
Mod
, with Gillespie-Hovey triple

(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\ \ {\rm H}_{A}(\mathcal{X})\oplus{\rm H}_{B}(_{B}\mathcal{I}),\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{W}\\
B\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X})/_{A}\mathcal{I}\cong{\rm Ho}(A)
.

7.4.
Projective (Injective) models on Morita rings

An abelian model structure on (abelian) category
\mathcal{A}
is
projective
(respectively,
injective
) if
each object is fibrant (respectively, cofibrant), i.e.,
the Hovey triple is of the form
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y})
(respectively,
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{X})
). See [H2], [Gil2].

The following observation clarifies the relation between projective (respectively, injective) models and gpctps (respectively, gictps).

Lemma 7.11
.

([Bec, 1.1.9]; [Gil3, 1.1])
Let
(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
be a complete cotorsion pair in abelian category
\mathcal{A}
with enough projective objects and enough injective objects. Then

(1)

(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{A},\ \mathcal{Y})
is a
(
hereditary
)
Hovey triple if and only if

(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
is a generalized projective cotorsion pair.

(1^{\prime})

(\mathcal{A},\ \mathcal{Y},\ \mathcal{X})
is a
(
hereditary
)
Hovey triple if and only if

(\mathcal{X},\ \mathcal{Y})
is a generalized injective cotorsion pair.

Any gpctp
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
in
B
-Mod gives compatible gpctps

(_{B}\mathcal{P},\ B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod})
and
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
.
Any gictp
(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
in
B
-Mod gives compatible gictps

(\mathcal{V},\ \mathcal{Y})
and
(B\mbox{-}{\rm Mod},\ _{B}\mathcal{I})
.
Thus, by Theorem
7.9
one gets:

Corollary 7.12
.

Let
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&N\\
M&B\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be a Morita ring with
M\otimes_{A}N=0=N\otimes_{B}M
. Suppose that
N_{B}
is flat and
{}_{B}M
is projective.

(1)
Let
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{Y})
be a gpctp in
B
-
Mod
. Then

({\rm T}_{A}(_{A}\mathcal{P})\oplus{\rm T}_{B}(\mathcal{V}),\ \ \Lambda\text{\rm\rm-Mod},\ \ \left(\begin{smallmatrix}A\text{\rm\rm-Mod}\\
\mathcal{Y}\end{smallmatrix}\right))

is a hereditary Hovey triple, and
{\rm Ho}(\Lambda)\cong\mathcal{V}/_{B}\mathcal{P}
.

(2)
Let
